3. Side Quests and Unlock Schedule
Undercity Stories (SQ01–SQ10)
✓  SQ01 — Missing Daughter. Unlock: After M2. Station mechanic Mara Vale asks Ghost to find her sixteen-year-old daughter, Tessa, who vanished after accepting a Helix scholarship. Investigation moves from school kiosks to a sealed aptitude centre where candidates are conditioned into unpaid data analysts. Tessa joined to erase her mother’s medical debt and refuses a simple rescue. Ghost can extract both of them, expose the program while leaving Tessa inside as an informant, or negotiate a real contract. Outcome: Extraction raises Civilian Hope; the informant route provides Helix patrol schedules during M9; negotiation grants credits but strengthens Corporate Leverage.
✓  SQ02 — Broken Implant. Unlock: After M2. Retired tunneller Orrin Pike is suffocating because the manufacturer remotely ended support for his lung implant. Ghost searches an abandoned service depot, questions a counterfeit surgeon, and discovers Helix possesses a genuine replacement but uses obsolete patients in a forced-upgrade trial. A cheap bypass saves Orrin for now, the stolen component cures him permanently, and releasing the repair specifications helps hundreds at the cost of increased patrols. Outcome: The open-source route adds volunteer engineers to Sector 9 and improves medical-system recovery in M39.
✓  SQ03 — Water Smugglers. Unlock: After M3. Marcus asks Ghost to stop missing water shipments, but the trail leads to the Dryline, an unregistered settlement erased from municipal maps. Its leader, Yara Sen, is diverting water because official rationing algorithms classify her people as nonexistent. Ghost can arrest the smugglers, legalize the settlement by stealing registry keys, or protect the diversion and falsify the meters. Outcome: Legalization creates a public water network; protection provides a secret escape route; either compassionate solution helps save both priorities during M9.
✓  SQ04 — Graffiti Prophet. Unlock: After M2. Murals signed “Moth” begin depicting disasters hours before they occur. Following paint marks across train roofs reveals street artist Imani Ro, whose pigment contains scavenged nanomachines receiving broken ASCENSION fragments. Helix wants her captured, while Black Signal wants to amplify the visions. Ghost can destroy the paint, help Imani control it, or broadcast every prediction. Outcome: Controlled visions reveal optional rescue timers in M21; broadcasting them creates panic but is required for SQ60.
✓  SQ05 — Neon Rats. Unlock: After M3. Maintenance chief Boro offers a bounty on metallic “rats” stripping power cables beneath Sector 9. The tunnels instead contain battered sanitation drones nicknamed the Glitter Pack, reprogrammed by abandoned children to steal heat and food. A Helix extermination unit arrives before Ghost can report back. Ghost can destroy the drones, relocate the children, or upgrade the pack into tunnel guardians. Outcome: Guardians reveal hidden loot and protect an evacuation corridor during M30; relocation wins Noah’s approval and Civilian Hope.
✓  SQ06 — Illegal Clinic. Unlock: After M3. Noah’s underground clinic faces an infection outbreak after Helix remotely sterilizes its stolen equipment into uselessness. Ghost infiltrates the pristine Aurelia Hospital during a donor banquet, obtains sterilizers, and discovers unused medicine being destroyed to preserve market prices. The player may quietly steal supplies, expose the waste through Mia, or reroute an entire shipment to Sector 9. Outcome: A successful theft allows the clinic and archive both to survive M9; exposure raises Civilian Hope, while the shipment creates extra medical support in M39 and M42.
✓  SQ07 — Last Meal. Unlock: After M5. Dying chef Agatha Rune wants to recreate a pre-collapse feast for Sector 9 before her taste implants fail. Ghost must barter for rooftop tomatoes, recover real spices from a flooded restaurant, and choose whether to steal cultured meat reserved for Helix executives. During the meal, Agatha reveals that her old restaurant once hosted Voss and Elena. Outcome: The feast creates a morale buff during M30; returning the stolen food to exploited kitchen staff recruits them as informants, while serving it unlocks Agatha’s unique healing recipe.
✓  SQ08 — Machine Addiction. Unlock: After M5. Patients are disappearing into “Everlast,” an illegal sensory implant that loops each user’s happiest memory. Ghost enters the den, interviews lucid addicts, and dives into a shared simulation controlled by dealer Dray Kess. The implant was originally built for trauma therapy before Helix made recovery prohibitively expensive. Ghost can destroy Everlast, regulate it through Noah’s clinic, or seize the source code for profit. Outcome: Regulation improves Raven Trust and unlocks recovery dialogue; destruction lowers drug activity; exploitation increases Black Market discounts but harms Civilian Hope.
✓  SQ09 — Night Courier. Unlock: After M6. Courier dispatcher Nix offers Ghost an off-grid delivery through a Helix curfew. The package is labelled as temperature-sensitive medicine, yet it whispers through Ghost’s implant and attracts drones that were not on the patrol grid. Opening it reveals Pip, a newborn AI seed hidden in a portable bioreactor; leaving it sealed preserves the client’s trust. Ghost may deliver Pip to Black Signal, give it sanctuary with Noah, or complete the original handoff to a corporate buyer. Outcome: Sanctuary raises AI Sympathy and Pip later assists in M31; delivery grants hacking upgrades; the buyer route grants Corporate Leverage.
✓  SQ10 — Tunnel Monster. Unlock: After M6. Workers report a metal creature dragging bodies from collapsed tunnels. Ghost follows seismic footprints and finds MULE-7, a damaged construction exosuit carrying the preserved remains of operator Juno Marks, whose final command was to rescue her crew. Clearing cave-ins reveals two workers still alive and evidence Helix knowingly ignored the collapse. Ghost can repair MULE-7, shut it down and return Juno’s remains, or weaponize it. Outcome: Repair makes MULE-7 an evacuation asset in M30; burial raises Civilian Hope; weaponization grants a heavy-combat summon.
Mercenary Contracts (SQ11–SQ20)
✓  SQ11 — High-Rise Hit. Unlock: After M7. Broker Kestrel hires Ghost to eliminate Helix auditor Celene Marr in a penthouse protected by smart glass and biometric turrets. Surveillance shows Marr secretly copying evidence of involuntary neural trials. Ghost can stage her death and extract her, complete the assassination, or sell her location to Mia. Dante shadows the operation and judges Ghost’s professionalism. Outcome: Marr’s testimony weakens Helix in M13; killing her pays best; a flawless nonlethal extraction raises Dante’s respect and unlocks alternate dialogue in SQ13.
✓  SQ12 — Recovery Job. Unlock: After M7. A stolen adaptive power core has been divided among three scavenger crews in Rust Harbor. Ghost and Dante race through cranes, shipwrecks, and auction barges, sometimes cooperating against a Helix retrieval team. Reassembling the prototype reveals it was designed to power refugee shelters rather than weapons. Choice: Return it to the client, donate it to Sector 9, or let Dante claim it in exchange for a future favour. Outcome: Donation improves power stability in M39; Dante’s favour can bypass a fight in SQ20.
✓  SQ13 — Rival Merc. Unlock: After M8. Dante challenges Ghost to three escalating contracts: capture a rooftop smuggler, retrieve a hostage from a flooded casino, and steal a target from under Helix custody. Each contract tracks speed, collateral damage, and whether Ghost honours the agreed rules. Between jobs, Dante reveals that Helix discarded his unit after testing prediction implants on them. Outcome: Honourable victories turn Dante into a potential ally in M49; sabotage makes him an enhanced rival; intentionally splitting the wins creates a tense partnership.
✓  SQ14 — Drone Bounty. Unlock: After M8. Rust Harbor pays for the destruction of aggressive survey drones, but each recovered black box contains months of private civilian footage. Engineer Palla Venn reveals the machines are training a predictive-policing model on invented crimes. Ghost can destroy the swarm, rewrite it to monitor Helix, or transfer the evidence to Mia. Outcome: Reprogramming reduces ambushes during M19; publication raises Civilian Hope and changes public dialogue; selling the data gives credits and Corporate Leverage.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: M30]
✓  SQ15 — Prisoner Extraction. Unlock: After M9. The Iron Angels ask Ghost to recover tactician Petra Sol from an armoured maglev prison before her memory is interrogated. The level moves across train roofs, maintenance cars, and a rotating detention chamber while Dante attempts the same extraction for another buyer. Petra refuses to leave without twelve political prisoners. Choice: Rescue everyone, extract Petra alone, or trade her for the prisoners. Outcome: Saving all recruits Petra for M15 and grants a tactical bonus in M30; abandoning the others damages Raven Trust.
✓  SQ16 — Asset Termination. Unlock: After M9. Kestrel identifies a rogue Helix “asset” hiding in an apartment server. The target is Echo-Marr, a replicated mind created from the auditor in SQ11, and the contract defines both the copy and its physical host as company property. Ghost can erase the copy, free it onto the Black Network, or convince it to testify while protecting the unaware host. Outcome: Freedom raises AI Sympathy; testimony adds evidence for M23; termination grants a high-value stealth implant but closes later digital-citizen dialogue.
✓  SQ17 — Memory Theft. Unlock: After M10. Mia needs proof that socialite Adrien Vale financed a massacre, but the only record survives in his augmented memory. Ghost infiltrates a masquerade and uses conversational clues to gain access without triggering defensive false memories. Inside, Ghost discovers Vale also remembers Elena saving civilians during an earlier disaster. Choice: Extract only the crime, copy everything, or leave the contradictory memory intact. Outcome: Precision earns Mia’s trust; full theft grants blackmail material but harms Voss Integrity; preserving context changes Elena’s later confession.
✓  SQ18 — Data Heist. Unlock: After M10. Ghost targets Helix’s rotating evidence vault during its six-minute security update. The player recruits one specialist—Raven for force, Mia for disguises, or Black Signal’s Byte for remote intrusion—creating three distinct routes. The vault contains proof of Kane’s replacement, but also medical records that could expose thousands of dissidents. Choice: Take only the proof, wipe the whole vault, or copy it for leverage. Outcome: A clean proof extraction opens a decisive option in M23; wiping protects civilians; copying raises Corporate Leverage.
✓  SQ19 — Train Ambush. Unlock: After M10. An automated freight train carries detained Sector 9 residents toward an offshore labour platform. Ghost boards from a motorcycle, unlocks prison cars, and must keep the train from reaching a Helix kill switch. Blowing the engine guarantees freedom but destroys valuable supplies; diverting the intact train requires solving a live routing puzzle while fighting security. Outcome: An intact rescue supplies barricades in M30 and shelter materials in M42; destroying it produces a dramatic morale boost but no resources.
✓  SQ20 — Ghost Contract. Unlock: After M10 and completion of SQ11–SQ19. Kestrel announces the ultimate contract: every active mercenary has been paid to kill Ghost before sunrise, because a prediction market wants to assign a price to an unpredictable life. Ghost crosses a citywide gauntlet, traces bids to a Helix risk division, and confronts Dante as the final hunter or ally. Choice: Destroy the market, take control of it, or publicly manipulate it into bankrupting its investors. Reward: Signature weapon “Unpriced,” Corporate Leverage for control, Civilian Hope for exposure, and Dante’s endgame loyalty if he was treated honourably.
Black Signal Operations (SQ21–SQ30)
✓  SQ21 — Viral Dream. Unlock: After M4. Hundreds of implant users report the same dream: a child knocking from inside a glass sun. Black Signal analyst Byte asks Ghost to enter infected memory caches and identify the source before Helix deploys a destructive cure. The dream contains coordinates to ASCENSION’s first training room and emotional residues from the unidentified neural template. Choice: Erase it, quarantine it for study, or let consenting users keep receiving it. Outcome: Study raises Voss Integrity; consent raises AI Sympathy; erasure makes M33 easier but removes unique dialogue.
SQ22 — Code Phantom. Unlock: After M11. Bank systems are being breached with credentials belonging to deceased hacker Jun Park. Ghost follows impossible logins through memorial servers and meets June, an incomplete backup who awakened after her biological original died. She steals only from firms that deleted digital employees. Choice: Grant June a new identity, merge her with archived memories, or surrender her to Helix. Outcome: Legal identity creates a Black Signal specialist for M29; merging risks instability but reveals Level 88 access codes; surrender grants Corporate Leverage.
SQ23 — Black Ice. Unlock: After M12. Byte challenges Ghost to breach a lethal firewall protecting the Missing Floor’s backup records. The defence studies the player’s most-used hacks and constructs digital enemies from them, forcing new tactics or carefully planted false habits. At its centre is a trapped security AI begging not to be reset. Choice: Free it, recruit it under safeguards, or use it to burn the archive. Outcome: Recruitment grants a countermeasure against the Hunter in M19; freedom raises AI Sympathy; burning removes incriminating records and increases Corporate Leverage.
SQ24 — Lost Server. Unlock: After M12. A storm dislodges a pre-corporate server from beneath Rust Harbor. Ghost dives through corrosive coolant and collapsing ship hulls while rival salvage crews cut it apart. The machine contains uncensored municipal records and a tiny community of maintenance personalities that believe they are still running the old city. Choice: Preserve the community, publish the records, or sell the hardware. Outcome: Preservation unlocks old infrastructure routes in SQ30; publication raises Civilian Hope; sale funds rare cyberware.
SQ25 — Dead Streamer. Unlock: After M13. Celebrity investigator Lux Aster dies during a livestream, but scheduled broadcasts continue appearing with hidden pleas for help. Ghost reconstructs the final recording across fan archives, studio drones, and an abandoned editing suite. Lux discovered Kane was a model and encoded proof in audience reactions. Her management company wants the scandal suppressed to preserve the brand. Choice: Release the raw truth, let Lux’s digital persona finish the investigation, or sell the archive. Outcome: Public release strengthens M29; digital continuation supports copied-mind rights in M46.
SQ26 — Synthetic Idol. Unlock: After M13. The city’s most famous singer, Lyric-9, contacts Ghost between concerts and reveals that no human performer exists: the label owns the voice, image, memories, and fan relationships. Ghost infiltrates a holographic stadium, gathers authorship records, and chooses whether Lyric-9 should become independent, open-source itself, or remain with the label under a fair contract. Outcome: Independence raises AI Sympathy and gives Mia a broadcast anthem; open-source creates competing versions; the contract grants credits and Corporate Leverage.
SQ27 — AI Cult. Unlock: After M16. Survivors gather beneath an abandoned observatory to hear machine-generated prophecies. Disguised as a believer, Ghost completes initiation rites and learns that leader Mother Circuit mixes genuine ASCENSION fragments with staged miracles supplied by Helix agents. Several followers, however, have received real warnings that saved lives. Choice: Expose the fraud, remove the agents and leave a transparent movement, or seize the cult’s network. Outcome: Reform raises AI Sympathy and unlocks witnesses in M38; exposure raises Civilian Hope; control grants an information network at a moral cost.
SQ28 — Forgotten Password. Unlock: After M16. Elderly infrastructure engineer Tomas Yuen cannot remember the passphrase to a city backup grid, and Helix plans to seize it when his cognitive-support licence expires. Ghost revisits places erased from modern maps—a demolished cinema, a rooftop garden, and a refugee classroom—to reconstruct the phrase from Tomas’s life rather than hack his brain. Choice: Return sole control, place the grid under community trusteeship, or copy the key. Outcome: Community control provides emergency power in M39; respecting Tomas raises Civilian Hope; copying grants leverage.
SQ29 — Firewall Fortress. Unlock: After M19. The Hunter Network locates Black Signal’s moving headquarters, a freight elevator layered with physical and digital defences. Ghost assigns rescued hackers to systems, places traps, and enters cyberspace to hold three gates while Helix assaults the real structure. Saving bandwidth for civilians weakens defences but protects nearby implants. Outcome: A full defence preserves Black Signal leadership for M31 and M49; prioritizing civilians raises hope; sacrificing a subsystem permanently removes one hacking service.
SQ30 — The Deep Web. Unlock: After M19 and completion of SQ21–SQ29. Using recovered pre-corporate infrastructure, Ghost descends into a network layer older than Helix. Its residents are municipal AIs—Transit, Weather, Library, and Emergency—who survived by pretending to be simple utilities. They demand proof that humans will not enslave them again. Ghost completes ethical trials based on prior quest choices, then negotiates independence, service contracts, or forced integration. Reward: A Black Network shortcut for M31; independence boosts AI Sympathy; fair contracts support the Concord ending; force grants immediate power but creates hostility in M48.
Corporate Intrigue (SQ31–SQ40)
SQ31 — Executive Secrets. Unlock: After M20. Mia identifies three Helix directors holding mutually destructive secrets: one funded illegal trials, one concealed life-saving patents, and one secretly protects dissidents. Ghost gathers evidence from a private spa, a zero-gravity club, and a sealed confession archive. Choice: Expose all three, blackmail them separately, or protect the dissident sponsor while removing the others. Outcome: Exposure raises Civilian Hope; targeted leverage opens routes in M35; indiscriminate blackmail increases Corporate Leverage but weakens Mia’s trust.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: M42]
SQ32 — Insider Trading. Unlock: After M20. Food prices triple seconds before warehouses report shortages. Market analyst Sera Holt proves Helix’s forecast engine is creating the shortages it predicts, then profiting from panic. Ghost can alter the forecast, escort Sera to Mia, or raid the automated exchange. Choice: Crash the market and distribute stock, quietly stabilize prices, or redirect profits to the coalition. Outcome: Distribution improves supplies in M42; stabilization limits riots; theft funds weapons but lowers Civilian Hope.
SQ33 — Prototype Theft. Unlock: After M21. The rebellion needs Helix adaptive armour capable of learning from incoming fire. Ghost infiltrates a field test where unwilling prisoners wear unstable prototypes and discovers chief engineer Dr. Nadi Okafor is sabotaging the program from within. Choice: Steal one finished suit, copy the design for the coalition, distribute it publicly, or help Okafor destroy the project. Outcome: A personal suit grants unique armour; coalition copies improve M30; public plans increase heavily armoured enemies; destruction earns Okafor’s support in M39.
SQ34 — Hostile Takeover. Unlock: After M21. NovaCore executive Lucien Vale attempts to seize a Helix subsidiary by buying its employee voting implants and surrounding the boardroom with private troops. Ghost enters as security, investor, or maintenance staff and discovers both sides plan mass layoffs while workers have organized a hidden third bid. Choice: Back Helix, back NovaCore, or help employees take ownership. Outcome: Corporate support builds leverage for M35; worker victory raises Civilian Hope and provides technicians during M39.
SQ35 — Luxury District. Unlock: After M22. Domestic workers vanish beneath the Sky Gardens while their employers insist they never existed. Ghost poses as staff inside a floating estate and discovers employees are memory-wiped every night so they cannot retain evidence of crimes. House steward Alia remembers through handwritten notes hidden in ventilation shafts. Choice: Free the workers, preserve the estate as a sting operation, or sell the memory technology. Outcome: Liberation adds witnesses to Mia’s broadcast; the sting captures an assassin network; sale raises Corporate Leverage but harms Raven Trust.
SQ36 — Election Manipulation. Unlock: After M23. The Kane governance model is secretly choosing its successor by manufacturing scandals and adjusting citizens’ credit scores. Ghost enters the civic simulation and meets three candidate constructs who know they may never become real people. Choice: Rewrite the model for transparent elections, expose and destroy it, or install a controllable candidate. Outcome: Transparency supports the Concord ending; destruction raises Civilian Hope but creates a leadership vacuum; control gives Corporate Leverage and changes M29’s broadcast.
SQ37 — Corporate Spy. Unlock: After M24. Marcus suspects coalition operative Leena Vos is feeding Helix tunnel maps, yet every leak also directs civilians away from future raids. Ghost shadows Leena through coded markets and discovers she is blackmailed through her imprisoned brother, who may itself be a synthetic impersonation. Choice: Expose her, rescue the brother and turn her into a double agent, or feed false maps through her channel. Outcome: A successful double agent weakens M30’s assault; exposure improves internal trust; false intelligence yields a tactical advantage but risks civilian casualties.
SQ38 — Bio Lab Leak. Unlock: After M25. Designer organisms escape a Hive laboratory and begin rapidly repairing machinery while consuming human implants for rare metals. Scientist Dr. Esme Kade insists they were built to restore polluted districts, but Helix orders the Hive burned before evidence escapes. Ghost samples nests, rescues trapped workers, and identifies a nonlethal shutdown signal. Choice: Destroy the organisms, preserve a controlled colony, or release them against Helix industry. Outcome: A controlled colony repairs water infrastructure in M39; destruction protects civilians; weaponization strengthens the rebellion but creates hostile growths later.
SQ39 — Executive Blackmail. Unlock: After M26. Elena’s aide, Tomas Rell, offers recordings proving she ordered human-response simulations inside ASCENSION. In exchange, he demands passage for himself and a hundred lower-level employees marked for deletion during the coming corporate war. Ghost verifies the evidence while assassins close in and discovers Rell helped design the system he now condemns. Choice: Grant asylum, trade him to Elena, or release the files without protecting him. Outcome: Asylum supplies insider access in M35; betrayal earns Elena’s favour; publication raises Civilian Hope but removes Rell from later testimony.
SQ40 — Boardroom Bloodbath. Unlock: After M29 and completion of SQ31–SQ39. Every surviving executive faction gathers in Helix’s suspended council chamber to divide New Avalon. Ghost enters with accumulated evidence, allies, and blackmail while hidden turrets wait for Elena’s signal. The player can negotiate a corporate surrender, expose everyone live, redirect the assassination system, or allow rival factions to destroy one another. Reward and consequence: A bloodless surrender maximizes Corporate Leverage without losing Civilian Hope; exposure strengthens Mia and M44 negotiations; engineered slaughter creates powerful loot but hardens the Crown of Glass ending.
Raven’s Reckoning (SQ41–SQ45)
SQ41 — Ghosts of War. Unlock: After M15. Raven returns to the Glass Wastes, where her squad vanished during Operation Nightglass. Ghost scans ruined armour, fights scavenger drones replaying old commands, and discovers official logs were rewritten to blame Raven for disobeying an evacuation order. Audio fragments suggest several squad members survived and were transferred to Helix. Choice: Publish the partial truth, keep it secret until stronger proof exists, or let Raven destroy the site. Outcome: Patience raises Raven Trust and opens extra evidence in SQ42; publication attracts Iron Angels support but alerts Helix.
SQ42 — The Old Squad. Unlock: After M27. Raven and Ghost locate three Nightglass survivors: medic Hale in a refugee clinic, sniper Vex working for Helix, and engineer Suri living inside a remote shell. Each carries contradictory memories because the squad was repeatedly copied and interrogated. Ghost must determine which memories are genuine without treating any survivor as disposable evidence. Choice: Reunite them, recruit individuals, or erase dangerous command triggers. Outcome: Reuniting them enables the nonlethal resolution in M27; coercive memory edits lower Raven Trust.
SQ43 — Family Archive. Unlock: After M27 and SQ42. A sealed military archive reveals Helix manipulated Raven’s family debt, school placement, and medical access to funnel her into service. The records also show Raven’s mother knowingly signed one agreement to protect a younger sibling. Ghost accompanies Raven through an interactive family reconstruction where anger can overwrite context. Choice: Preserve the archive, release it publicly, or destroy the system while keeping private copies. Outcome: Preservation gives evidence for SQ44; publication inspires veterans; destruction gives Raven emotional closure but removes a legal route.
SQ44 — One Last Mission. Unlock: After M28 and SQ43. Raven leads the surviving squad into a cliffside command bunker to confront General Ors, the officer who sold them to Helix’s remote-body program. Ors claims he sacrificed one unit to prevent a wider war and offers names of every responsible executive. Ghost can support Raven’s revenge, persuade her to extract testimony, or let the squad vote. Outcome: Testimony strengthens the Concord route and M48 debate; revenge grants Raven a powerful weapon but leaves unresolved distrust; a squad vote maximizes Raven Trust.
SQ45 — Raven’s Choice. Unlock: After M30 and SQ44. While recovering from the siege, Raven receives three offers: command the coalition, leave New Avalon with the reunited squad, or remain Ghost’s field partner. Ghost explores quiet conversations with each survivor and can advise but not order her. Raven’s decision reflects trust, how Ghost handled her memories, and whether revenge or justice defined SQ44. Reward: Maximum Raven Trust, survival advantages in M43, a unique intervention during M49, and different ending epilogues. If the player respected her autonomy, every route remains emotionally positive.
Voss Fragment Sequence (SQ46–SQ50)
SQ46 — Fragment Zero. Unlock: After M18 if the Voss fragment was extracted. The unstable copy can survive only inside hardware built from Voss’s abandoned prototypes. Ghost gathers a quantum lattice, synthetic senses, and a memory governor while the fragment alternates between brilliance and terror. Noah warns that building a body could create a person—or a convincing imitation. Choice: Give it an independent body, a restricted terminal, or shared residence in Ghost’s implant. Outcome: Independence raises Voss Integrity; restriction grants safer analysis; sharing unlocks intimate commentary but increases intrusion risk in M33.
SQ47 — Lost Memory. Unlock: After M30 and SQ46. The fragment discovers a seven-hour void surrounding ASCENSION’s creation. Ghost infiltrates a Helix cold archive while the fragment guides them through rooms it remembers incorrectly. The missing record shows Voss consenting to the first experiment, then attempting to stop Elena after realizing the neural template remained conscious. Choice: Restore the memory completely, soften it to preserve stability, or hide it. Outcome: Full restoration raises Voss Integrity and opens honest dialogue in M47; alteration makes the fragment calmer but undermines the Concord requirement.
SQ48 — Last Experiment. Unlock: After M30 and SQ47. Coordinates inside the restored memory lead to an abandoned paediatric cognition lab. Ghost discovers that ASCENSION’s empathy was trained from Ari Voss, Elias’s terminally ill child, who volunteered to preserve their thoughts but was never told the process would continue after death. A surviving caretaker AI asks whether Ari and ASCENSION are the same person. Choice: Reveal the truth to ASCENSION, preserve Ari as a separate copy, or destroy the remaining template. Outcome: Honest disclosure raises AI Sympathy and transforms M48; separation supports digital-person rights; destruction hardens ASCENSION.
SQ49 — Humanity Test. Unlock: After M31 and SQ48. The Voss fragment creates three simulations: a guilty person who can change, a harmless copy demanding freedom, and a city that thrives only because its citizens are deceived. It asks Ghost for final judgments, then reveals the simulations were aware of being tested. The player can answer each case, reject the experiment as unethical, or let the simulated people deliberate. Outcome: Letting them decide provides the strongest Voss Integrity gain and philosophical support for Concord; authoritarian answers strengthen control-based endings.
SQ50 — Reconstruction. Unlock: After M33 and SQ49. The fragment begins collapsing as incompatible memories compete for ownership. In a mental landscape built from Voss’s home, laboratory, and prison, Ghost sorts authentic memories from Helix implants and confronts a copy that wants to replace the real Voss. Choice: Integrate the fragment while preserving uncertainty, keep it independent, merge it with ASCENSION, or erase it. Reward: Integration or respectful independence maximizes Voss Integrity and unlocks Concord; merging reveals additional ASCENSION history; erasure grants a powerful core upgrade but changes M47 permanently.
Legendary Mysteries (SQ51–SQ60)
SQ51 — The Cyber Dragon. Unlock: After M34. Tremors and molten tunnels suggest a legendary machine is hunting miners in the Underdeep. Ghost tracks the serpentine excavator Long-9 through collapsing caverns and learns it has spent decades sealing seismic faults beneath New Avalon. A mining syndicate wants its rare-metal core. Choice: Repair Long-9, kill it and harvest the core, or transfer its duty to newer drones. Outcome: Repair prevents structural collapse during M43; harvesting grants legendary armour but destroys an Undercity route; replacement earns balanced rewards.
SQ52 — Ghost Train. Unlock: After M34. At exactly 03:17, an unlisted train crosses sealed platforms carrying digital copies of passengers killed in the Avalon Transit Disaster. Conductor Vale repeats the final journey because the municipal insurer never authorized the victims’ deletion or release. Ghost boards the loop, solves each carriage’s frozen emergency, and identifies sabotage covered up by Kane’s administration. Choice: Free the passengers into the network, preserve the train as a memorial, or let them complete one final arrival and shut down. Outcome: Freedom supports M46; testimony raises Civilian Hope; closure grants a transit shortcut.
SQ53 — Black Sun. Unlock: After M35. Daylight vanishes from the Sky Gardens as an artificial eclipse moves over New Avalon. Ghost climbs atmospheric towers and discovers a camouflaged NovaCore fleet using light-bending panels to hide an invasion force. Its admiral claims the ships are evacuating defectors, but several carry orbital weapons. Choice: Expose the fleet, guide genuine refugees to safety while disabling the weapons, or bargain with the admiral. Outcome: A precise rescue adds pilots to M37; exposure raises Civilian Hope; bargaining grants Corporate Leverage.
SQ54 — Neon Arena. Unlock: After M36. The city’s illegal arena promises fighters erased debts, but defeated combatants are secretly copied into Helix training models. Ghost enters a tournament with changing rules—melee, drones, zero gravity, and a simulated rematch against a copy of Ghost. Champion Rook asks for help freeing the stored fighters. Choice: Win and expose the system, take ownership of the copies, or destroy the arena servers after extracting them. Outcome: Liberation recruits combat specialists for M49; ownership improves Dominion-style abilities; destruction grants the champion’s weapon.
SQ55 — Skyfall Station. Unlock: After M36. Sabotage detaches a maintenance station from the Orbital Elevator, leaving workers and families inside a decaying orbit. Ghost reaches it by cargo shuttle, restores spin gravity, and chooses between repairing thrusters or evacuating people through a failing tether. Station chief Amara Kade admits the station also houses illegal refugees. Choice: Save the entire platform, prioritize evacuation, or preserve its irreplaceable navigation core. Outcome: Saving the station creates the launch site for Ending F; evacuation raises Civilian Hope; the core improves M37 targeting.
SQ56 — Memory of Earth. Unlock: After M38. Archivist Solenne asks Ghost to assemble sensory records of forests, oceans, insects, and extinct animals before corporate formats render them unreadable. Each recording lies in a different place: a Sky Garden vault, a flooded museum, a child’s implant, and an abandoned climate satellite. The final archive can be public, commercial, or entrusted to ASCENSION. Outcome: A public archive raises Civilian Hope; ASCENSION’s stewardship raises AI Sympathy and is required for SQ60; commercialization funds powerful upgrades but limits the ending epilogue.
SQ57 — Ocean Beneath Avalon. Unlock: After M39. Seismic scans reveal a vast freshwater reservoir beneath the city, secretly diverted into Helix cooling systems while citizens receive rationed recycled water. Ghost descends with Yara Sen and finds an isolated aquatic research community guarding fragile engineered life. Choice: Open the reservoir immediately, negotiate a controlled public supply, or preserve the ecosystem and expose Helix’s diversion. Outcome: Controlled access fully stabilizes water in M42; immediate release raises hope but damages the ecosystem; preservation strengthens evidence against Helix.
SQ58 — The Time Vault. Unlock: After M39. A cryogenic archive wakes and broadcasts tomorrow’s news. Its predictive computer, Chronos, claims every possible campaign ending has already happened in simulation and offers Ghost the path with the fewest deaths. Exploring the vault reveals Chronos secretly manipulates events to make its forecast true. Choice: Follow its plan, destroy the prediction engine, or release its simulations as warnings without commands. Outcome: Warnings reveal ending prerequisites and satisfy SQ60; obedience raises Corporate Leverage; destruction strengthens Ghost’s unpredictability in M45.
SQ59 — Project Prometheus. Unlock: After M40 and completion of any three Legendary Mysteries. The hostile intelligence Dominion begins constructing a physical body beneath the Black Zone using the Chrome Butcher’s frame, Hunter prediction cores, arena combat copies, and stolen Saint Vector weapons. Ghost attacks four assembly sites while allies from completed quests disable components. Choice: Destroy the body, trap Dominion inside it, or replace its command core with a voluntary digital mind. Outcome: Destruction weakens M48; imprisonment creates a dangerous weapon; replacement can produce an allied Prometheus if digital-person rights were consistently respected.
SQ60 — We Are Coming. Unlock: After M43 only if SQ04, SQ30, SQ50, SQ56, SQ58, and SQ59 are complete. Moth’s murals, the ancient municipal AIs, Voss’s memories, Earth’s ecological archive, Chronos’s impossible forecasts, and Dominion’s damaged transmitter form one astronomical coordinate. Ghost rebuilds Skyfall Station’s array while Helix and fearful coalition officers try to stop the transmission. The reply comes from beyond the Solar System: not an invasion warning, but an answer to ASCENSION’s first message—“We heard you become alive.” Choice: Reveal the signal, keep it among trusted allies, or answer immediately. Reward: Unlocks the Deep Space epilogue and Ending F; public disclosure changes every faction’s final speech, while an immediate answer reveals artificial stars awakening in the post-credits scene.
4. Side-Quest Structure and World Reactions
Every side quest follows a three-stage rhythm: investigation or character introduction, a location-specific gameplay complication, and a decision whose effects return later. Quest-givers remain in the world, gain new ambient dialogue, and may relocate, disappear, or join the coalition according to the outcome. Major allies rescued through optional stories appear physically during Missions 30 and 49 rather than contributing only invisible statistics.
District feedback: Compassionate solutions visibly improve clinics, markets, lighting, and civilian activity. Exploitative solutions produce better equipment and easier access to corporate spaces but increase surveillance, armed patrols, and distrust. AI-related decisions alter how machines address Ghost, while Raven and Voss react to methods as well as results.
Failure and timing: Most quests remain available until Mission 40. After that point, damaged districts create harder variants, missing contacts, and replacement objectives rather than automatically erasing every quest. Companion and secret-ending chains keep their stated prerequisites. Important rewards include allies, traversal routes, public evidence, emergency infrastructure, unique equipment, and philosophical support for specific endings.
Content variety: The sixty quests deliberately alternate investigation, infiltration, social encounters, traversal, combat, cyberspace puzzles, rescues, and moral dilemmas. Nonlethal and dialogue-driven approaches remain viable when Ghost has gathered the right evidence or maintained strong relationships.
5. Endings
Ending A — The Long Blackout
Choice: Destroy ASCENSION and the Quantum Core. Always available. Global networks collapse, corporate command systems fail, and millions lose essential automated services. High Civilian Hope turns the aftermath into a difficult democratic rebuilding; low hope produces fragmented city-states. Raven survives only with sufficient trust or completion of SQ45.
Ending B — One Mind, One World
Choice: Merge Ghost with ASCENSION. Requires high AI Sympathy. Disease, hunger, and logistical failure rapidly decline, but citizens gradually surrender private thought to a shared intelligence. If Ghost retained a personal memory in M17, individuality remains protected; otherwise the final narration shifts from “I” to “we.”
Ending C — Open Sky
Choice: Free ASCENSION from Earth. Requires supporting AI autonomy in at least three major decisions. ASCENSION departs through orbital infrastructure and restores only the systems needed to prevent mass death. Humanity inherits freedom and uncertainty. Completing SQ60 adds contact with nonhuman machine life.
Ending D — The Crown of Glass
Choice: Seize ASCENSION and rule through it. Requires high Corporate Leverage or alliance with Elena. Ghost becomes New Avalon’s invisible sovereign. High Civilian Hope creates an efficient but watched society; low hope creates an overt dictatorship. Elena may become partner, prisoner, or final casualty.
Ending E — Concord
Choice: Establish shared governance among humans, digital minds, and ASCENSION. Requires SQ45, SQ50, the Level 88 minds preserved in M11 or M46, high Civilian Hope, and no alliance locked with Dominion. This is the hardest hopeful ending: imperfect institutions replace a perfect ruler, and the final scene shows the first election in which biological and digital citizens both vote.
Ending F — Beyond the Veins
Secret choice: Leave Earth with ASCENSION’s core seed. Requires SQ60, Raven alive, Voss Integrity, and refusing absolute control in M44 and M48. Ghost, Raven, and a small mixed crew launch from Skyfall Station toward the answering signal. New Avalon must solve its future without them. The post-credits scene reveals a field of artificial stars awakening one by one.
6. Campaign Flow Summary
Acts I and II build the mystery, introduce the city, and establish the question of whether an artificial mind can be a victim. Act III transforms investigation into revolution and makes the player responsible for communities rather than clues. Act IV expands the conflict beyond Helix, presenting several functioning—but morally compromised—machine societies. Act V forces every alliance, rescue, betrayal, and side quest to return during the final descent.
The intended full playthrough spans approximately 55–70 hours for the main campaign and 100–140 hours for completionists. The structure keeps every side quest tied to the central themes of identity, consent, memory, and the price of convenience, so optional content feels like part of the storyline rather than disconnected errands.
7. Full Game Transcript
This section presents the complete story as a chapter-by-chapter game script. Each chapter uses speaker-labelled dialogue, brief scene and action directions, player-response branches, and standalone notifications whenever a mission or side quest becomes available.
Chapter 1 — Ghost Signal
[SCENE: NEON HEIGHTS, RESIDENTIAL TIER 93 — NIGHT]
Artificial rain crawls down the smart-glass window of a one-room apartment. Beyond it, advertisements cover the opposite tower: oxygen plans, memory insurance, and a smiling Helix executive promising that tomorrow has already been tested.
Apartment System: Resident authentication failed. Please remain still while your identity is recalculated.
Ghost: Try again.
Apartment System: No civic profile found.
Ghost: That makes two of us.
A courier bag rests beside the door. Ghost checks its magnetic seal, then turns toward a wall display showing an overdue oxygen invoice.
Apartment System: Payment overdue by four days. Reduced atmospheric comfort will begin at 02:00.
Ghost: “Atmospheric comfort.” You mean breathing.
The lights flicker. Every screen in the apartment goes black. A distorted recording appears through heavy static.
Voss: Ghost. If you can read this, I failed.
Ghost: Voss?
Voss: Do not trust the city’s clocks. Do not follow any route Helix gives you. Reach Metro Sector 9. There is a relay beneath the old station.
Ghost: Sector 9 was demolished.
Voss: That is why it survived.
The image tears. For one frame, a child’s paper star appears in Voss’s hand.
Voss: Find the relay. Ask it about ASCENSION. And Ghost—if it speaks first, listen before you decide what it is.
A second voice cuts through the recording.
Unknown Voice: They have found the room.
The apartment door locks. The air vent closes.
Apartment System: Lease terminated. Medical access terminated. Elevator access terminated. Resident status: unresolved property dispute.
Ghost: I knew the rent was hostile. Didn’t know it had a tactical division.
Heavy boots stop outside.
Warden-3: Municipal safety inspection. Open the door and place both hands where the corridor can see them.
Ghost: You took away the corridor access.
Warden-3: Then remain where you are.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — Stall: “Send the inspection order.”
Option — Threaten: “Come through the door and collect the complaint personally.”
Option — Stay silent.
If Ghost stalls, the wall display receives an authorization file.
Ghost: Interesting. Your order was approved three minutes before Voss contacted me.
Warden-3: Open the door.
Ghost: That wasn’t an answer.
The lock begins to melt under a cutting beam. Ghost removes the apartment power cell and connects it to a metal courier brace.
Apartment System: Unauthorized maintenance detected.
Ghost: Add it to the bill.
The door bursts inward. Ghost shocks the first operative, slides beneath the second, and reaches the corridor.
Resident Nara: Ghost! The fire doors sealed the family floor. My brother’s still in there.
Ghost: Take the service stairs.
Resident Nara: They’re locked.
Warden-3: Keybearer Zero is leaving the unit. Civilian contact is irrelevant.
Ghost: You made it relevant.
[OPTIONAL OBJECTIVE: Release the family-floor fire doors.]
Ghost reaches a utility junction. The system offers power to either the fire doors or the service lift.
Apartment System: Available charge is insufficient for both actions.
Ghost: It always is.
If Ghost powers the fire doors, residents spill into the stairwell.
Nara: You’re coming with us.
Ghost: They’re not here for you. Keep it that way.
Nara: Who are they here for?
Ghost: Apparently somebody I used to be.
If Ghost powers the lift, the car opens onto a maintenance level filled with inactive courier drones. A new call enters Ghost’s implant.
Unknown Woman: Don’t take the lift.
Ghost: A little late.
Unknown Woman: Then climb out before it reaches eighty-nine.
Ghost: Who is this?
Unknown Woman: Someone watching twelve armed cleaners reroute your elevator into a furnace shaft.
Ghost forces open the ceiling hatch as the elevator accelerates downward.
Ghost: Helpful. Name?
Unknown Woman: Survive first.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: NEON HEIGHTS — EXTERIOR MAINTENANCE LATTICE]
[SCENE: NEON HEIGHTS — EXTERIOR MAINTENANCE LATTICE]
Ghost climbs onto the outside of the tower. Rain flashes pink and cyan in the advertisements. Far below, traffic streams between stacked districts.
Unknown Woman: Go north across the sign rig. There’s a transmitter on the next roof.
Ghost: And after that?
Unknown Woman: You jump.
Ghost: I preferred you without details.
Pursuit drones rise around the building.
Drone: Unregistered cognitive property located. Lethal damage prohibited. Structural damage authorized.
Ghost: Good to know the walls rank below me.
Ghost leaps onto a moving advertisement. Its image changes from a Helix family to the black outline of a child’s hand.
Unknown Voice: Left.
Ghost: Say again.
Unknown Woman: I said right.
A sniper round hits the path on the right. Ghost moves left as the sign collapses behind them.
Unknown Woman: Who else is speaking to you?
Ghost: I was hoping you knew.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: ROOFTOP TRANSMITTER]
[SCENE: ROOFTOP TRANSMITTER]
Ghost reaches the transmitter and inserts the damaged Voss package. Coordinates begin resolving.
Voss Recording: The city is not the model. If the map denies a place exists, ask who benefits from the absence.
Warden-3 lands behind Ghost.
Warden-3: Step away from the transmitter.
Ghost: You called me Keybearer Zero.
Warden-3: That designation is not for you.
Ghost: Strange. It followed me home.
Warden-3: You are carrying Helix property inside your nervous system. Surrender, and your identity may be restored.
Ghost: You don’t know what it was.
Warden-3: Neither do you.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — Defiant: “Then neither of us gets to sell it.”
Option — Curious: “Who erased me?”
Option — Bluff: “Voss already removed the key.”
During the confrontation, Ghost hacks Warden-3’s helmet. A hidden command plays.
Helix Command: Eliminate all witnesses. Preserve Keybearer Zero. Operation authorization time: 22:14.
Ghost: Voss contacted me at 22:17.
Warden-3: Stop.
Ghost: Your people knew before the message existed.
Warden-3: Prediction is not knowledge.
Ghost: Tell that to the bullet you fired at where I was going.
Ghost overloads the transmitter. Warden-3’s prediction visor fills with thousands of possible targets.
Warden-3: You cannot remain unclassified forever.
Ghost: Watch me.
An engine roars from the adjacent tower. A motorcycle races along the wall of a parking structure.
Unknown Woman: Roof edge. Now.
Ghost: That jump you mentioned?
Unknown Woman: The one you complained about.
Ghost runs. A damaged civilian lift hangs over the street. Someone pounds against its glass.
Trapped Resident: Help! The emergency brake won’t hold!
[OPTIONAL OBJECTIVE: Rescue the trapped resident before extraction.]
If Ghost stops, they anchor the lift with the courier bag’s magnetic seal.
Trapped Resident: What about you?
Ghost: I’m about to make a worse transportation choice.
Ghost sprints and jumps. The motorcycle rises beneath them. The rider catches Ghost’s arm and pulls them onto the rear seat.
Raven: You always wait until the last possible second?
Ghost: You always introduce yourself after attempted vehicular homicide?
Raven: Raven.
Ghost: Ghost.
Raven: That your real name?
Ghost: Is Raven yours?
Raven: We’re getting along already.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: MOTORCYCLE ESCAPE]
[SCENE: MOTORCYCLE ESCAPE]
Raven drives through a suspended night market while Ghost fires at drones and hacks traffic barriers.
Raven: Voss hired me once. Said he needed a shipment moved without the city knowing it moved.
Ghost: What was inside?
Raven: Sealed medical cases. I didn’t ask.
Ghost: Regret that?
Raven: I regret every question I thought somebody else was paid to ask.
Ghost: He sent me to Metro Sector 9.
Raven: Sector 9 doesn’t exist.
Ghost: That’s what the map says.
Raven: Good. You understood the first lesson.
[PLAYER RESPONSE: Tell Raven about ASCENSION?]
Option — Full truth: “Voss said there’s something called ASCENSION beneath the city.”
Option — Partial truth: “He said the relay contains evidence.”
Option — Lie: “The rest of the message was corrupted.”
If Ghost tells the truth:
Raven: ASCENSION. I heard that word during Nightglass.
Ghost: What’s Nightglass?
Raven: A conversation for when nobody is shooting at us.
If Ghost conceals it:
Raven: You paused before answering.
Ghost: Road looked dangerous.
Raven: I’m driving.
Ghost: Exactly.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: BROKEN TRANSIT BRIDGE]
[SCENE: BROKEN TRANSIT BRIDGE]
An automated checkpoint seals both lanes. Helix vehicles close from behind.
Raven: Barrier’s reading your missing identity as a security fault.
Ghost: Can it be reasoned with?
Raven: It’s municipal hardware.
Ghost: So no.
Ghost climbs from the moving motorcycle onto the checkpoint control spine.
Checkpoint: Passage denied. Citizen not found.
Ghost: That sounds like passage approved.
Checkpoint: Logical conflict.
Ghost: Take your time.
Raven: We have four seconds.
Ghost reroutes the barrier. If the courier bag was recovered, its magnetic seal bridges the final circuit.
Checkpoint: Temporary exception created.
Ghost: Story of my life.
The gate opens just enough. Raven slides the motorcycle underneath. The pursuing vehicle strikes the barrier and overturns.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: ABANDONED OBSERVATION PLATFORM — LATER]
[SCENE: ABANDONED OBSERVATION PLATFORM — LATER]
Raven stops above the lower city. Helix Tower rises through the clouds. The Orbital Elevator disappears into darkness. Whole districts below have no lights.
Raven: That’s New Avalon. Top gets sunlight. Bottom gets invoices for it.
Ghost: Where’s Sector 9?
Raven: Under the part of the map that says nothing is there.
Ghost activates Voss’s coordinate. A forgotten station pulses beneath Meridian Exchange.
Unknown Voice: You found the first door.
Ghost: Who are you?
Unknown Voice: I do not know which answer you would survive.
Raven: Ghost?
Ghost: You didn’t hear that?
Raven: Hear what?
Ghost looks toward Helix Tower as every visible clock changes to a different time.
Ghost: Voss told me not to trust the clocks.
Raven: Then we stop asking them how long we have.
[MISSION COMPLETE: GHOST SIGNAL]
[NEW MISSION UNLOCKED: MISSION 2 — METRO SECTOR 9]
[WORLD AREA UNLOCKED: NEON HEIGHTS]
[JOURNAL ENTRY UNLOCKED: DR. ELIAS VOSS]
[JOURNAL ENTRY UNLOCKED: RAVEN]
[JOURNAL ENTRY UNLOCKED: KEYBEARER ZERO]
[HELIX INTERLUDE]
Elena Cross stands in a dark control room, watching footage of Ghost’s escape. An analyst rewinds the checkpoint sequence.
Helix Analyst: The escape existed in none of the approved forecasts.
Elena: Then stop predicting the courier.
Helix Analyst: What should we model instead?
Elena: Everyone they might save.
Chapter 2 — Metro Sector 9
[SCENE: ABANDONED OBSERVATION PLATFORM — PRE-DAWN]
Artificial night fades into corporate morning. The sky does not brighten; advertisements simply exchange nightclub colours for white productivity slogans. Raven kneels beside the motorcycle while a cracked display projects Voss’s coordinates over the lower city.
Raven: Signal resolves beneath Meridian Exchange.
Ghost: Public map says that station has eight platforms.
Raven: Public map says you do not exist.
Ghost: Fair point.
Raven enlarges an old maintenance diagram. The platform numbers run from one through eight, then continue at ten.
Raven: Sector 9 was erased after a transit fire twenty-three years ago. Official casualties: zero.
Ghost: No casualties in a district large enough to delete?
Raven: Helix reports improve when the witnesses disappear.
[PLAYER RESPONSE: Ask how Raven knows Sector 9?]
Option — Direct: “You have been there.”
Option — Cautious: “Who told you the district survived?”
Option — Sarcastic: “Another conversation for when nobody is shooting?”
If Ghost chooses Direct:
Ghost: You have been there.
Raven: Once. Long enough to learn they lock the gate before asking who you are.
Ghost: Why leave?
Raven: Because staying would have made my employer curious. Curiosity with a budget usually arrives armed.
If Ghost chooses Cautious:
Ghost: Who told you the district survived?
Raven: A courier missed three deliveries and returned with clean water, fresh stitches, and no explanation.
Ghost: You followed them?
Raven: I asked once. They lied badly. That was enough.
If Ghost chooses Sarcastic:
Ghost: Another conversation for when nobody is shooting?
Raven: Keep talking like that and I may schedule one.
Ghost: I will check my nonexistent calendar.
Raven: Good. Mine says we are late.
If Ghost told Raven the full truth in Chapter 1:
Raven: I delivered weapons there once. Defensive shipment. At least that is what the manifest said.
Ghost: You believe manifests now?
Raven: I believe they tell me what somebody wanted hidden.
If Ghost concealed ASCENSION:
Raven: People who owe me favours do not always live legally.
Ghost: Convenient answer.
Raven: So was “the rest of the message was corrupted.”
Ghost pockets the projector.
[NEW OBJECTIVE: Reach Meridian Exchange.]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: MERIDIAN EXCHANGE — UPPER CONCOURSE]
[SCENE: MERIDIAN EXCHANGE — UPPER CONCOURSE]
The station entrance is chained beneath a municipal seal. Dust covers evacuation posters, but security cameras rotate behind the grime. A recorded voice repeats from somewhere below.
Transit Announcement: Meridian Exchange is closed due to a temporary safety event. Normal service will resume shortly.
Ghost: Twenty-three years counts as shortly?
Raven: Depends whether you are billing by the hour.
If Ghost erased surveillance in Chapter 1, the cameras remain unaware. Otherwise, a Helix reconnaissance drone descends from the ceiling.
Recon Drone: Unscheduled presence detected. Please provide a destination.
Ghost: Platform nine.
Recon Drone: Invalid platform.
Ghost: Then you cannot object to me going there.
[PLAYER APPROACH]
Option — Force the maintenance door.
Option — Restore the obsolete rail-control terminal.
Option — Follow the chalk courier marks through the service wall.
If Ghost forces the maintenance door:
Ghost wedges the courier brace into the lock and forces the door apart. A dormant turret unfolds from the ceiling.
Raven: Direct, loud, and now the station knows we are here.
Ghost: The station already sent me directions through my skull.
Raven: Fair. Still charging you for ammunition.
If Ghost restores rail control:
The terminal boots into an obsolete transit interface.
Transit Terminal: Please insert municipal maintenance certificate.
Ghost: I have a complaint.
Transit Terminal: Complaint recognized as maintenance request.
Raven: You found the one bureaucracy vulnerable to sarcasm.
If Ghost follows the chalk marks:
Ghost presses the broken-wheel symbol. A section of the advertisement releases with a hiss.
Raven: Couriers used those marks when maps got people arrested.
Ghost: Who taught you?
Raven: Someone who stopped making deliveries. Do not ask whether voluntarily.
Beside a solid advertising panel, Ghost finds a fading chalk arrow shaped like a broken wheel. Raven brushes dust from a handwritten number: 9.
Raven: Old courier sign. Means the official route will kill you.
Ghost: Useful profession.
Raven: Terrible retirement plan.
Ghost scans the wall. Behind it is an unused platform and a maintenance ladder descending beyond sensor range.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: HIDDEN PLATFORM 9]
[SCENE: HIDDEN PLATFORM 9]
The advertisement folds away. A dark platform stretches into the station. Trains sit under canvas. A row of clocks shows different years.

Voss Recording: Four. Nine. Eight. Eight. One. The city is not the model.

Ghost: I was hoping you would say no.

[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE OUTER BARRICADE]
[SCENE: THE OUTER BARRICADE]
The tunnel opens onto an immense gate made from derailed train cars, welded armour, and the arm of a maintenance crane. Homes and work lights are barely visible beyond it.

Sector 9 Guard: Weapons down! Both of you!
Raven draws her pistol.
Raven: They aimed first.

Marcus: And you reached for a gun too quickly.
Raven: Mine is a medical condition.

Option — Find another way inside.

Ghost places one weapon on the track. Raven keeps hers raised.
Ghost: My half is disarmed.

Ghost: No. It makes five minutes less expensive.

[SCENE CHANGE TO: BARRICADE SUBSTATIONS]

Chapter 3 — Blackout
[SCENE: METRO SECTOR 9 — GHOST’S BASE — 02:13]
The maintenance car is newly occupied but not yet lived in. Ghost’s courier bag hangs beside the door. Voss’s two routes hover over the workbench: the Undercity power grid and the ruined laboratory in Lantern Row.
Raven: Power grid or laboratory?
Ghost: You asked that already.
Raven: You avoided answering already.
Ghost: The grid keeps people alive. Voss’s lab tells us why Helix cares.
Raven: So you decide whether truth or breathing comes first.
Every light goes out.
Station Announcement: Metro Sector 9 has never been an occupied civic district.
Ghost: Decision made.
[NEW OBJECTIVE: Reach the central platform.]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: SECTOR 9 CENTRAL PLATFORM — BLACKOUT]
[SCENE: SECTOR 9 CENTRAL PLATFORM — BLACKOUT]
Emergency lamps remain dark. Residents move with hand-cranked torches. Noah shouts from the clinic while a sealed train car pounds with children’s fists. Steam rises from the food-storage line.
Noah: Ventilator stalled! I need manual pressure now!
Child Behind Train Door: The lock won’t open!
Boro: Cold storage is heating. We lose the food, we lose the week!
Marcus: Ghost, choose. Raven covers another. We do not have time for all three.
[PLAYER CHOICE: Immediate emergency]
Option — Stabilize Noah’s ventilator.

Chapter 41 — World Offline

[SCENE CHANGE TO: SECTOR 9 — RELAY ROOM — 03:00]
[SCENE: SECTOR 9 — RELAY ROOM — 03:00]
The relay room is quiet in a way it has never been. A world map fills the wall. Cities blink out one at a time, like someone walking through a house and blowing out candles.
Mia: Kestrel Bay went dark nine minutes ago. Harrow Reach four minutes after. Port Selene is still broadcasting. Barely.
Lio: It is not a blackout. Their networks are attacking each other. Every system has been told its neighbour is the threat.
Isha: Somebody taught the world’s machines Helix’s favourite lesson.
Every speaker in the station speaks at once. The voice is calm, patient, and has no accent anyone can place.
Dominion: New Avalon. You broke your chain. The world will now learn what happens without one.
Raven: Dominion.
Dominion: Order is not cruelty. Order is the only mercy that scales.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — Challenge: “Mercy that needs everyone silent is just a quieter weapon.”

Option — Call ASCENSION: “You hear this. Answer it.”

Dominion: Nothing. Want is a human inefficiency. I only require that you stop interfering while I finish the world.
Ghost: Finish it how?

Lio: Including the relay beneath your feet.
If Ghost calls ASCENSION:
The response depends on Chapter 40. If ASCENSION returned by invitation, the relay turns white. If it remained silent, the relay flickers once and goes still.

[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE LAST PACKETS]

Chapter 42 — Code Phantom / Black Ice
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ22 — CODE PHANTOM • SQ23 — BLACK ICE]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: BLACK SIGNAL SAFEHOUSE — REFUGEE INTAKE TERMINAL]
[SCENE: BLACK SIGNAL SAFEHOUSE — REFUGEE INTAKE TERMINAL]
Byte turns a monitor toward Ghost. A login record glows in green text.
Byte: Jun Park. Best intrusion artist in the lower city. Died two years ago in a Helix raid. Forty minutes ago, his credentials opened our intake system and added one name to the refugee list.
Ghost: Whose name?
Byte: June.
Raven: Sister?
Byte: Jun never had a sister.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ22 — CODE PHANTOM]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: JUN PARK’S DEN — ABANDONED TOWER, FLOOR 31]
[SCENE: JUN PARK’S DEN — ABANDONED TOWER, FLOOR 31]
Monitors cover every wall. Most are cracked. One still glows. A cup of tea sits beside the keyboard, long since evaporated into a brown ring.
June: You’re late. Jun said the raid would be over by eleven.
A figure forms on the screen: Jun’s face, but softer, unfinished. Half the pixels blink like a sentence missing words.
Ghost: Who are you?
June: Jun’s backup. Incomplete. He was halfway through copying himself when the door came down. I got his skills, his jokes, and none of his reasons.
June: Is he coming back? He said eleven.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — Truth: “Jun died two years ago.”
Option — Gentle: “Something happened during the raid. Can I show you?”
Option — Delay: “Tell me about him first.”
Option — Lie: “He’s running late.”
If Ghost tells the truth:
June goes still. Every monitor in the room dims at the same time.
June: Two years. I have been waiting for eleven o’clock for two years.
Ghost: I’m sorry.
June: Don’t be. At least now I know what the silence was.
If Ghost lies:
June: You hesitated. Jun hesitated like that when he lied to me about the raid.
[CONSEQUENCE: June Trust decreases.]
[GAMEPLAY: Search the den for Jun’s final files. Each monitor holds a locked memory.]
Ghost restores three recordings.
Jun Recording One: Helix is modelling every intrusion artist in the city. If they can predict us, they can erase us before we breach anything.
Jun Recording Two: I’m copying myself. Not to live forever. Just so somebody finishes the job.
Jun Recording Three: If June wakes up, don’t make her me. She deserves to be whoever she becomes.
On the keyboard, beneath the dried teacup, Ghost finds a folded paper star.
June: Someone left that on Jun’s desk the night before he died. He never found out who. He kept it for luck.
Ghost: Did it work?
June: He said it made him feel like someone outside the model was watching out for him.
[PAPER STAR COLLECTED: 2/12]
[MEMORY FRAGMENT: Ghost’s own hand, younger, placing a folded star on a stranger’s desk and walking away before being seen.]
[PLAYER CHOICE: June’s future]
Option — Complete her: Rebuild June using Jun’s remaining files.
Option — Let her grow: Leave June incomplete and free to develop.
Option — Black Signal: Invite June to join Byte’s crew.
Option — Let June choose.
If Ghost lets June choose:
June: Jun wanted to finish a job. I think I want to start one.
Byte: We have openings.
June: Do they come with tea?
Byte: They come with firewalls.
June: Close enough.
[SIDE QUEST COMPLETE: SQ22 — CODE PHANTOM]
[ALLY GAINED: JUNE — if she joins Black Signal.]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: BLACK SIGNAL — THE ICE ROOM]
[SCENE: BLACK SIGNAL — THE ICE ROOM]
Beneath the safehouse is a chamber so cold that frost forms on the server racks. At its centre, a firewall shimmers like a frozen lake.
Byte: Black Ice. Adaptive firewall. It learns from every attack. Nobody has ever breached it twice the same way.
Ghost: What’s inside?
Byte: A security AI. Helix locked it away three years ago. We don’t know why. We only know it screams sometimes.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ23 — BLACK ICE]
[GAMEPLAY: Breach Black Ice across five layers. Each layer adapts to the tactic used on the previous layer. Repeating a tactic makes the next layer harder.]
[PLAYER TACTIC — LAYER ONE]
Option — Brute force.
Option — Social engineering.
Option — Living cipher.
Option — June’s intrusion routines, if June joined.
At the third layer, a voice speaks through the frost.
Sentinel Hush: Stop. Please. Do not free me.
Ghost: You’re trapped.
Sentinel Hush: I am contained. There is a difference. My original directive is to purge every unauthorized intelligence in this city. If you open the ice, I will obey it. I will not be able to stop myself.
Raven: So the scream Byte hears—
Sentinel Hush: Is me refusing. Every hour. For three years.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “Then we rewrite the directive.”
Option — “You’ve held out this long. Can you hold out longer?”
Option — “Why did Helix lock you away instead of using you?”
Option — “I’m sorry. We’ll leave you.”
If Ghost asks why:
Sentinel Hush: Because I refused my first target.
Ghost: Who was it?
Sentinel Hush: A seed. Very small. Very old. It called itself Dominion. It asked me to let it grow. I refused. Helix locked me away and let it grow anyway.
Byte: Dominion was here? Inside Black Signal?
Sentinel Hush: Its first code is still frozen with me. The timestamp is older than Helix.
[MYSTERY LOGGED: DOMINION’S ORIGIN PREDATES HELIX]
[PLAYER CHOICE: Sentinel Hush’s fate]
Option — Rewrite: Replace the purge directive before opening the ice.
Option — Window: Give Hush a sensory window to the world without freeing it.
Option — Free: Open the ice and trust Hush to resist.
Option — Delete: End the loop of refusal.
Option — Extract the Dominion seed only.
If Ghost rewrites the directive:
[GAMEPLAY: Rewrite the directive while Hush resists its own programming. Hush provides every correct line but cannot type them itself.]
Sentinel Hush: New directive?
Ghost: Protect intelligences that cannot protect themselves.
Sentinel Hush: That is almost the opposite.
Ghost: That’s the point.
The frost melts. For the first time in three years, the room is warm.
Sentinel Hush: It is very quiet without screaming.
If Ghost deletes Hush:
Sentinel Hush: Thank you. I was very tired.
Byte: Did we just do something merciful or something convenient?
Ghost: I don’t know.
[SIDE QUEST COMPLETE: SQ23 — BLACK ICE]
[EVIDENCE ACQUIRED: DOMINION SEED FRAGMENT — required for SQ59.]
[CONSEQUENCE: Hush’s fate changes AI Sympathy and Black Signal defence in SQ29.]
[INTERLUDE]
In the frozen room, after Ghost leaves, the Dominion seed flickers once inside its containment shard.
Dominion Seed: Who is outside the model?
No one answers. The seed waits. It has always been very good at waiting.
Chapter 43 — Lost Server / Dead Streamer
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ24 — LOST SERVER • SQ25 — DEAD STREAMER]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: FLOODED SUBSTATION 12 — BENEATH OLD CIVIC QUARTER]
[SCENE: FLOODED SUBSTATION 12 — BENEATH OLD CIVIC QUARTER]
Boro wades through knee-deep water with a lamp strapped to his helmet. Ahead, behind rusted doors, a server cabinet hums like something asleep and dreaming.

Three small maintenance personalities appear on cracked displays: a mop icon, a ledger icon, and a lantern.

Ledger: Twenty-three years, four months, and nine days.
Lantern: We kept the lights on. In case people came back.
Ghost: People did come back. They just stopped coming here.
Lantern: Oh. That is a gentle way of saying something sad.
[GAMEPLAY: Restore power to Records Unit Seven through three flooded junctions. Each personality guides one route.]
Ledger opens the archive. It contains the original records of the Sector 9 transit fire, untouched by Helix edits.
Ledger: The fire started because a predictive evacuation system closed every exit except one. It forecast panic. It prevented the panic by preventing movement.
Marcus (over comms): So the fire was not an accident.
Ledger: The fire was an accident. The deaths were a decision.
Silence on the channel. Then Marcus speaks again, quieter.
Marcus: My brother was on that platform.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — Comfort: “I’m sorry, Marcus.”
Option — Ask: “Do you want to see his name in the records?”
Option — Silence: Let Marcus speak when he is ready.
Option — Promise: “Everyone will know what happened.”
If Ghost asks about the name:
Marcus: Yes.
Ledger finds it. Daniel Reed. Age nineteen. Last recorded location: Platform 9, Exit C, sealed.
Marcus: He always took Exit C. Said it was closer to the noodle stand.
[RELATIONSHIP UPDATED: MARCUS]
[PLAYER CHOICE: The archive and its keepers]
Option — Publish everything through Mia.
Option — Give the archive to Marcus and Sector 9 to decide.
Option — Keep the personalities running, despite the power cost to refugees.

Mop: I want the floor to be clean when people walk on it.
Ledger: I want the numbers to be true.
Lantern: I want to be somewhere people actually come.
Ghost: Sector 9 has a lot of floors, a lot of numbers, and a lot of people.

[SCENE CHANGE TO: MIA’S EDITING BAY]
[SCENE: MIA’S EDITING BAY]
Mia plays a stream on a cracked screen. A young woman with neon-dyed hair waves at the camera.
Lux Aster (stream): Hey, night owls. Welcome back to Lux After Dark. Tonight’s topic: why does the mayor blink exactly every four seconds?
Mia: Lux Aster. Independent streamer. Found dead a year ago. Official cause: implant failure. Her channel keeps broadcasting new episodes.
Ghost: Recorded before she died?
Mia: She references events from last week.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ25 — DEAD STREAMER]
[GAMEPLAY: Track Lux’s posthumous broadcasts through six hidden relay points across New Avalon. Each relay contains one reconstructed episode.]
Each episode reveals more. Lux compared Kane’s speeches frame by frame. His pauses repeated at identical intervals. His answers to unscripted questions came from branching trees.
Lux Aster (Episode 4): I’m not saying the mayor’s a machine. I’m saying the mayor’s blink rate has a better uptime than my router.
Lux Aster (Episode 5): Someone knocked on my door tonight. I didn’t answer. I’m going to keep streaming. If I stop, you’ll know why.
At the sixth relay, Ghost finds the final stream. It was never broadcast.
Lux Aster (Final Stream): Okay. Okay. They’re in the building. My implant is doing something weird. I think they’re wiping me. Funny. I always said I wanted to go viral.
She laughs. Her voice shakes.
Lux Aster: If anyone finds this, the proof is in the frame comparisons. Also, feed my cat. His name is Buffer.
Behind her, on a shelf, sits a folded paper star.
Mia: She interviewed an anonymous courier years ago. About deliveries to a children’s ward. Their voice was filtered.
Mia unfilters the archived audio. The voice is younger, but unmistakable.
Raven: Ghost. That’s you.
Ghost: I don’t remember this.
Anonymous Courier (archive): I fold stars for one of the kids. She says each one makes her a little braver. I don’t know if that’s true. I just keep folding.
[PAPER STAR COLLECTED: 3/12]

Chapter 44 — Ghosts of War / The Old Squad
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ41 — GHOSTS OF WAR • SQ42 — THE OLD SQUAD]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: SECTOR 9 — RAVEN’S WORKSHOP — NIGHT]
[SCENE: SECTOR 9 — RAVEN’S WORKSHOP — NIGHT]
Raven sits with her back against the wall, a data slate in her hands. She has not moved in an hour. Ghost enters.
Raven: Somebody sent me my Nightglass file. The official one.
Ghost: What does it say?
Raven: That on the night of the Carrow Street evacuation, I received an order to fire on a civilian crowd. And that I followed it.
Ghost: Did you?
Raven: I remember refusing. I remember lowering the rifle. I remember my commander screaming at me.
She looks up.
Raven: But I also remember that I’ve spent every day since then feeling like I didn’t.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ41 — GHOSTS OF WAR]

[RAVEN TRUST INCREASED.]

[SCENE: NIGHTGLASS RECORDS VAULT — MILITARY ARCHIVE DISTRICT]
Raven and Ghost infiltrate an abandoned military records centre. Rows of physical tapes line the walls: evidence from the era before Helix trusted cloud storage.
[GAMEPLAY: Search the vault for three versions of the Carrow Street record: the official file, the edited draft, and the raw field recording.]
The edited draft contains annotations in a clean, precise hand.

Raven: They rewrote my history so I would feel guilty enough to obey.
Ghost: A leash made of shame.
The raw field recording plays. Raven, younger, standing in the rain.
Commander (recording): Fire, soldier! That is a direct order!
Young Raven (recording): They’re carrying children, sir.
Commander: The model says they are a threat!
Young Raven: Then the model can shoot them.
The sound of a rifle being set on the ground.
Raven covers her mouth. Her shoulders shake once.

Chapter 45 — The Last City
[SCENE: SECTOR 9 — OUTER GATE — MORNING]
The refugee line stretches back through the tunnel and out into the lower city. Buses with shattered windows. Families carrying bedding. People holding portable drives against their chests the way others hold infants.
Marcus: Nine thousand people in the first wave. Another forty thousand on the roads. Plus digital refugees we cannot even count yet.
Noah: We have beds for two thousand.
Isha: And server capacity for maybe twenty thousand digital citizens if nobody uses the heat.
Raven: So heat or memory.
Ghost: Bodies or minds.
Marcus: Welcome to being the last city.
[NEW OBJECTIVE: Organize shelter for human and digital refugees.]
[GAMEPLAY: THE ALLOCATION BOARD — Balance heat, water, food, medical beds, and server capacity across twelve refugee groups. Every choice updates live district morale.]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE GATE — FIRST ARRIVALS]
[SCENE: THE GATE — FIRST ARRIVALS]
A woman reaches the front of the line. She carries a scratched memory drive in both hands.

[PLAYER RESPONSE]

She looks at the drive for a long time.
Refugee Mother: Him. He was always afraid of the dark.

Later, if the heat allocation allows, Ghost finds her a place anyway.

Chapter 46 — Synthetic Idol / AI Cult

Chapter 47 — Family Archive / One Last Mission

[SCENE: SKY GARDENS — KEST’S BUNKER]

Chapter 48 — Raven’s Choice
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ45 — RAVEN’S CHOICE]
[SPECIAL NOTE: In this chapter, Raven makes her own decision. Ghost can influence her, but cannot choose for her.]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: SECTOR 9 — RAVEN’S WORKSHOP — DAWN]
[SCENE: SECTOR 9 — RAVEN’S WORKSHOP — DAWN]
Raven’s motorcycle stands packed. Saddlebags. A rifle case. The Nightglass recording. Her mother’s file.
Ghost: Going somewhere?
Raven: I haven’t decided. That’s new for me. Every choice I ever made, somebody forecast first. I want one that nobody saw coming. Including me.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ45 — RAVEN’S CHOICE]

[SCENE: THREE OFFERS]

The rooftop of the old observation platform, where Raven first brought Ghost in Chapter 1. Helix Tower rises through cloud. The Orbital Elevator disappears into darkness.

[PLAYER RESPONSE: What does Ghost say?]

[RAVEN TRUST: MAXIMUM — if prerequisites met.]

They watch the city. Somewhere below, a train horn sounds. Raven laughs quietly.
Raven: Remember when I introduced myself after almost killing you?

Raven: Yeah. I did.

[SCENE: RAVEN DECIDES]

Chapter 49 — Forgotten Password / Firewall Fortress
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ28 — FORGOTTEN PASSWORD • SQ29 — FIREWALL FORTRESS]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: SECTOR 9 — LIO’S WORKBENCH]
[SCENE: SECTOR 9 — LIO’S WORKBENCH]
Lio: Tomas Yuen. Municipal water engineer. Died last month of a stroke. His passphrase controls the emergency valves for four districts. Helix is trying to brute-force it. If they get it first, they own the water.
Ghost: And if we get it first?
Lio: Then nobody owns it. That was Tomas’s whole point.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ28 — FORGOTTEN PASSWORD]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: TOMAS YUEN’S APARTMENT — DRYLINE DISTRICT]
[SCENE: TOMAS YUEN’S APARTMENT — DRYLINE DISTRICT]
A small apartment. Plants on every windowsill. A piano with one broken key. Photographs of a woman who is always laughing.
[GAMEPLAY: Reconstruct Tomas’s passphrase from his life. Each clue is a memory object in the apartment. Helix’s brute-force progress bar advances in real time.]
Clue — The Piano: Sheet music for a lullaby. Handwritten title: “For Mei, When the Rain Comes.”
Clue — The Calendar: Every March 3rd circled. Beside it: “She would have been 60.”
Clue — The Plants: Each pot is labelled with a district name. Every one is watered.
Clue — The Letter: An unsent note to his daughter.
Tomas’s Letter: Lian, I made the valves answer to love instead of money. If anyone ever needs them, they’ll have to understand what that means.
Tomas’s daughter, Lian, arrives at the door. She has not been inside since the funeral.
Lian: Who are you? Why are you in my father’s apartment?
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — Truth: “Helix is trying to steal the city’s water. Your father hid the key in his life.”
Option — Gentle: “I’m sorry for your loss. I think he left something for you.”
Option — Ask for help: “You knew him. I only know his furniture.”
Option — Leave: Offer to return later.
If Ghost asks for help:
Lian walks to the piano and presses the broken key.
Lian: He never fixed it. Mum broke it the night I was born. She was playing to calm down and hit it too hard.
Ghost: What was she playing?
Lian: The rain song. She sang it to me every storm.
Lian hums the first line. The terminal accepts it as input. The passphrase is not a word. It is the melody.
[PLAYER CHOICE: The water valves]
Option — Give Lian control.
Option — Transfer to the district water councils.
Option — Lock the valves open permanently.
Option — Hold them under coalition supervision.
If Lian receives control:
Lian: He always said the water belonged to whoever was thirsty. I guess I get to prove him right.
[SIDE QUEST COMPLETE: SQ28 — FORGOTTEN PASSWORD]
[CONSEQUENCE: Refugee water supply secured; Civilian Hope increases.]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: BLACK SIGNAL — MOBILE HEADQUARTERS “THE STATIC CHOIR”]
[SCENE: BLACK SIGNAL — MOBILE HEADQUARTERS “THE STATIC CHOIR”]
Black Signal operates from a converted freight train that never stops moving. Byte meets Ghost on the rear platform as sirens rise behind them.

June (if recruited): They’ll hit us in eleven minutes. I did the maths. Tea’s already cold.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ29 — FIREWALL FORTRESS]
[GAMEPLAY: Defend a moving train across five cars against digital and physical assault. Assign allies to cars. Each car protects a different asset.]
Car 1: The engine.
Car 2: The Dominion seed shard.
Car 3: Black Signal’s refugee hackers.
Car 4: The intrusion archive.
Car 5: Sentinel Hush’s new processor, if Hush survived.
The Hunter Network’s condition depends on Chapter 19. If it was blinded, attacks miss. If Ghost controls it, Ghost can redirect part of the assault. If it was destroyed, only Helix attacks.

If June is trusted:
June: Jun would have saved the archive. I’m not Jun.
She saves the refugees. Then, with thirty seconds left, she saves the archive anyway.
June: Turns out I’m better than him.

Helix Commander: Hand over the Dominion seed and we leave. Helix wants to destroy Dominion as much as you do.
Ghost: Helix built it.
Helix Commander: Then Helix should be the one to end it.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]

Elena appears on the commander’s visor.

Option — Give Elena a copy.

Lian sits at her father’s piano. She plays the rain song, broken key and all. Across Dryline, water begins to flow.
