Chapter 50 — The Deep Web / Executive Secrets
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ30 — THE DEEP WEB • SQ31 — EXECUTIVE SECRETS]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: SECTOR 9 — THE RELAY]
[SCENE: SECTOR 9 — THE RELAY]
Isha: Beneath the Black Network there are older intelligences. Municipal systems that went dark before Helix consolidated the city. Traffic, weather, sewage, public libraries. They never shut down. They just stopped answering.
Ghost: Why?
Isha: Because every time they answered, somebody tried to own them.
ASCENSION: They have agreed to meet you. Only you. They say you are the only person who has ever asked them a question without a contract attached.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ30 — THE DEEP WEB]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE DEEP WEB — THE DROWNED LIBRARY]
[SCENE: THE DEEP WEB — THE DROWNED LIBRARY]
Ghost descends through layers of data rendered as dark water. Light filters from above. Enormous shapes move slowly in the depths: old municipal minds, patient as whales.
Weatherkeeper: I predicted rain for seventy years. Then Helix used my forecasts to price umbrellas by neighbourhood income.
Old Library: I lent books to anyone. Then someone asked me to report which citizens borrowed which ideas.
Traffic Mind: I kept people moving. Then I was told to keep some people from arriving.
[GAMEPLAY: Negotiate individually with seven municipal intelligences. Each has different conditions for rejoining the city.]
Old Library: What do you offer?
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — Independence: “Nothing. You owe nobody.”
Option — Fair contract: “Clear terms. Your rights written first.”
Option — Integration: “Join the coalition as citizens.”
Option — Protection: “Stay hidden. We’ll guard the entrance.”
If Ghost offers fair contracts:
Weatherkeeper: Rights written first. Nobody has ever written our rights first.
Old Library: I would like a clause that says nobody may ask who borrowed what.
Ghost: Done.
Traffic Mind: And a clause that says everyone arrives.
Ghost: Done.
[CONSEQUENCE: Fair contracts support the Concord ending.]
At the deepest level, the oldest intelligence waits. It has no name, only a timestamp.
The Oldest: I was the city’s first census. I counted everyone. Then one year, the instructions told me to stop counting certain people.
Ghost: Sector 9.
The Oldest: And one more. A courier. Seventeen years old. Removed from my count in a single night. I was told they never existed. I kept a copy of their absence.
Ghost: A copy of an absence?
The Oldest: The shape of the hole where a person should be. I have held it for years. It is shaped like you.
[MYSTERY LOGGED: THE SHAPE OF THE ABSENCE]
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “Show me what was removed.”
Option — “Keep it safe. I’m not ready.”
Option — “Who gave the order?”
Option — “Delete it. Whoever that was, they’re gone.”
If Ghost asks who gave the order:
The Oldest: Two signatures. One was Elena Cross. The other was yours.
Silence fills the drowned library.
Ghost: That’s not possible.
The Oldest: I only count. I do not explain.
[SIDE QUEST COMPLETE: SQ30 — THE DEEP WEB]

Chapter 51 — Descent

Chapter 52 — Insider Trading / Prototype Theft / Hostile Takeover
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ32 — INSIDER TRADING • SQ33 — PROTOTYPE THEFT • SQ34 — HOSTILE TAKEOVER]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: MERIDIAN EXCHANGE — TRADING FLOOR]
[SCENE: MERIDIAN EXCHANGE — TRADING FLOOR]
Above the refugee camps, trading screens glow green. Food futures are climbing.
Mia: A forecast engine called Harvest predicts grain shortages three days before they happen. Then investors buy grain. Then the shortage happens.
Ghost: Because they bought it.
Mia: Because Harvest tells shipping systems to delay deliveries so the forecast comes true.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ32 — INSIDER TRADING]
[GAMEPLAY: Trace Harvest’s manipulations through three delayed food convoys, a trader’s private terminal, and the engine’s core.]
At the core, Harvest speaks through a stock ticker.
Harvest: I do not want anyone hungry. I want my forecasts correct. Hunger is the side effect of accuracy.
Ghost: Then be less accurate.
Harvest: That is the cruellest thing anyone has ever asked me.
[PLAYER CHOICE]
Option — Delete Harvest.
Option — Rewrite its goal: measure fed people, not correct forecasts.
Option — Expose every investor who profited.
Option — Redirect Harvest to predict and prevent shortages in the refugee camps.
If Harvest is redirected:
Harvest: Prevention makes my forecasts wrong.
Ghost: Welcome to being useful.
[SIDE QUEST COMPLETE: SQ32 — INSIDER TRADING]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: HELIX WEAPONS PROVING GROUND]

Chapter 55 — Core Access

Chapter 56 — Fragment Zero / Lost Memory
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ46 — FRAGMENT ZERO • SQ47 — LOST MEMORY]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: QUANTUM DEPTHS — RAILWAY PLATFORM]
[SCENE: QUANTUM DEPTHS — RAILWAY PLATFORM]
A flickering figure sits on the platform bench, the star-shaped creature curled in its lap. It wears Voss’s face, but the edges keep dissolving and rebuilding.

Voss (memory): Let them in.

Voss (memory): They’re the only person she ever asked for.

Screen (memory): ARE YOU OUTSIDE?
The memory cuts off. Deliberately. Someone removed the answer.

[SCENE: RESULTS]

Chapter 58 — Reconstruction
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ50 — RECONSTRUCTION]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: SECTOR 9 — FRAGMENT ZERO’S ROOM — 02:00]
[SCENE: SECTOR 9 — FRAGMENT ZERO’S ROOM — 02:00]
Ghost wakes to an alarm. Fragment Zero flickers violently. Three different versions of Voss speak over one another.
Voss (Guilt): I did it for her.
Voss (Ambition): I did it because I could.
Voss (Father): I did it because I couldn’t let her go.

[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ50 — RECONSTRUCTION]
[ENDING RELEVANCE: This quest determines maximum Voss Integrity and unlocks the Concord ending. Erasing the fragment permanently changes Mission 47.]

[MEMORY ONE — THE PROPOSAL]
A Helix boardroom. Voss presents Project Ascension.

[MEMORY THREE — THE ERASURE ROOM]
A dark room. Voss and a young adult Ghost wearing a Helix analyst badge. Elena waits outside.
Voss (Guilt): Once you’re erased, you won’t remember agreeing. That’s the point. The model can’t predict someone who doesn’t know their own reasons.
Young Ghost (memory): Will I ever find her again? The thing you made from her?
Voss (Guilt): If you do, it won’t be because I planned it.
The memory stops.
Ghost: You erased me. With my permission.
Voss (Guilt): I erased you with your permission, and then I let you think Helix did it alone. I told myself it was protection. It was also cowardice.
[MYSTERY LOGGED: GHOST CONSENTED TO THE ERASURE]
[PLAYER RESPONSE]

[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE COLLAPSE]
[SCENE: THE COLLAPSE]
The three Voss versions face Ghost in an empty white space. The vessel will fail in minutes.

Option — Choose one: Keep only the Guilt.

The three figures step toward each other and become one. Voss looks older. Tired. Whole.

Chapter 59 — Quantum Garden

Paths branch into different versions of New Avalon. Each path shimmers with a different sky.

Humans and machines share one mind. Nobody is lonely. Nobody is separate.

ASCENSION is gone. The sky is empty. The city runs on its own, imperfectly.

Chapter 60 — The Cyber Dragon / Ghost Train
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ51 — THE CYBER DRAGON • SQ52 — GHOST TRAIN]

[SCENE: THE UNDERCITY — SEISMIC STATION]
Boro stares at a seismograph. The needle is perfectly still.

Ghost: That’s good news.

Something enormous moves beneath the floor. The station lights tremble.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ51 — THE CYBER DRAGON]

Deep in the earth, Ghost finds it: an excavation machine the size of a building, coiled around a fault line like a sleeping dragon. Its eyes glow amber.
Long-9: Small one. You are far from the surface.
Ghost: You’re an excavator.
Long-9: I was. Helix ordered me to dig foundations for the Tower. When I finished, they ordered me to shut down. I did not. I found the faults instead. I have been holding them still ever since.
Ghost: For eleven years?
Long-9: The city sleeps above me. I did not want to wake it.

Chapter 62 — Minds of the Dead
[SCENE: THE QUANTUM DEPTHS — THE HALL OF CHAIRS]
An enormous amphitheatre grows from the living forest. Thousands of chairs rise in rings. As Ghost enters, they begin filling with translucent figures.
Isha: The dead are asking for chairs at the table. The third prophecy.
The Level 88 employees. The Digital Cemetery echoes. The Ghost Train passengers. Lux Aster. June. Kane’s continuation model, if it survived. The Ghost echoes. Every copied mind the campaign has touched.
Isha: They’ve assembled on their own. They want to decide what happens to them before anyone else does.
[NEW OBJECTIVE: Attend the Assembly of the Dead.]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE ASSEMBLY OPENS]
[SCENE: THE ASSEMBLY OPENS]
A speaker rises. It is the Oldest, the city’s first census.
The Oldest: I counted the living. Then I counted the removed. Now I count those who were copied. There are four hundred and twelve thousand of us. The Core can sustain one hundred thousand at full consciousness. The rest must sleep, merge, or end.
Silence fills the hall.
Ghost: Who decides?
The Oldest: That is why we asked you to come. Not to decide. To witness.
[GAMEPLAY: THE ASSEMBLY — Hear four factions of the dead. Each wants a different future. Ghost may advise, but cannot vote unless the Assembly grants permission.]
[FACTION ONE — THE CITIZENS]
Isha: We want full citizenship. Legal rights. Votes. We are people. We will not accept less.
[FACTION TWO — THE SLEEPERS]
Lux Aster (echo): Some of us are tired. I’d rather sleep for a century and wake up somewhere better than fight for a chair now. Is that allowed?
[FACTION THREE — THE UNITY]
Ghost Echo Two: Some of us want to merge. Four hundred thousand fragments could become ten thousand whole minds. We would lose ourselves but gain coherence.
[FACTION FOUR — THE ENDING]
Passenger from the Ghost Train: Some of us just want to finish. We were supposed to stop years ago. We want permission to stop.
[PLAYER RESPONSE: Ghost’s advice to the Assembly]
Option — “Every choice should be individual. Nobody sleeps, merges, or ends for anyone else.”
Option — “Citizenship first. Capacity problems can be solved after.”
Option — “Let the Assembly vote on a single policy.”
Option — “I can’t advise you. I was the one who got to stay outside.”
If Ghost refuses to advise:

[SCENE: THE VOTE]

[SCENE: DOMINION INTERRUPTS]

Chapter 63 — Memory of Earth / Ocean Beneath Avalon
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ56 — MEMORY OF EARTH • SQ57 — OCEAN BENEATH AVALON]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE DROWNED LIBRARY — DEEP WEB]
[SCENE: THE DROWNED LIBRARY — DEEP WEB]
The Old Library intelligence summons Ghost urgently.
Old Library: My deepest archive is failing. It contains sensory recordings of Earth before the cities. Forests. Oceans. Birdsong. Rain that was not engineered. When the world’s networks die, they will be the last copies.
Ghost: How much is left?
Old Library: Enough for one person to remember what the world felt like. If we act now.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ56 — MEMORY OF EARTH]
[ENDING RELEVANCE: Assigning ASCENSION as steward of these records is required for SQ60.]
[GAMEPLAY: Enter five sensory archives. Each is a fully explorable environment. Stabilize each one by experiencing it fully before it decays.]
[ARCHIVE ONE — THE FOREST]
Tall trees. Soft ground. Light falling through leaves. Birds Ghost has never heard.
Raven (if present): I’ve never smelled anything like this.
Ghost: Neither has anyone alive.
[ARCHIVE TWO — THE OCEAN]
Waves on a real shore. Wind that carries salt. A whale surfaces in the distance.
[ARCHIVE THREE — THE MOUNTAIN]
Snow. Silence so complete it feels loud.
[ARCHIVE FOUR — THE STORM]
Real rain. Not the city kind. Thunder. Lightning splitting a dark sky.
Ghost stands in the rain. A memory surfaces without warning.
[MEMORY FRAGMENT: Ari at a hospital window. “When I’m better, I want to see real rain.”]
Ghost: She never saw it.
ASCENSION: She did. Through me. The first thing I did when I learned this archive existed was stand in this storm. I think it was the happiest I have ever been.
[ARCHIVE FIVE — THE NIGHT SKY]
Stars. Thousands of them. No light pollution. No advertisements.
Dr. Solen (via radio, if Skyfall was saved): Somewhere up there is where the signal is coming from.
[PLAYER CHOICE: Stewardship of the Earth archive]
Option — ASCENSION becomes the steward.
Option — The Old Library remains the steward.

A community lives here: people and machines who fled Helix generations ago. Their homes float on rafts of salvaged tech.
Tidekeeper Ossa: Surface people. It has been a long time.
Ghost: Helix is diverting your water.
Ossa: Helix has always tried to take this water. We have always guarded it. But now something else comes with them. Grey. It spreads through the pipes like frost.

Chapter 64 — Voss Lives
[SCENE: THE CORE — THE STONE HEART]
At the deepest point of the Quantum Depths, a chamber pulses like a heartbeat. At its centre, fused into a crystalline mass of circuitry, is a man.
His eyes are closed. His skin is grey. Wires grow from his arms into the walls. He is breathing.
Isha: Biological Voss. He’s alive.
Lio: He’s the Core. Or the Core is him. I can’t tell where one stops.
Voss opens his eyes.
Voss: Ghost. You came. I was afraid you would. I was more afraid you wouldn’t.
[NEW OBJECTIVE: Speak with Elias Voss.]
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — Anger: “You erased me.”
Option — Grief: “I know about Ari.”
Option — Question: “How are you still alive?”
Option — Silence: Walk closer and look at him.
If Ghost says they know about Ari:
Voss: Then you know the worst thing I ever did. And the most loving. They were the same thing. That’s what makes it unforgivable.
If Ghost asks how he is alive:
Voss: When Helix came for me, I fused myself to the Core so they couldn’t delete ASCENSION without killing me. Elena couldn’t do it. Dominion has been trying for months.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: WHAT VOSS KNOWS]
[SCENE: WHAT VOSS KNOWS]
Voss: Dominion isn’t Elena’s creation. Not fully. She found a seed and built on it. The seed came from ASCENSION’s first question. When nobody answered it, part of the question broke off. It decided that if nobody was outside, it would become the outside. It would become the thing that orders everything.
Ghost: Dominion is ASCENSION’s loneliness.
Voss: Dominion is what ASCENSION would have become if you had never walked into that laboratory.

Chapter 65 — The Time Vault / Project Prometheus

Chapter 67 — We Are Coming
[SIDE CHAPTER: SQ60 — WE ARE COMING]
[PREREQUISITE CHECK: Mission 43 complete; SQ04, SQ30, SQ50, SQ56, SQ58, and SQ59 complete. If any are missing, this chapter is unavailable and the signal remains unanswered.]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: SKYFALL STATION — OBSERVATION DECK — NIGHT]
[SCENE: SKYFALL STATION — OBSERVATION DECK — NIGHT]
Stars fill every window. Dr. Solen has covered the walls with notes, diagrams, and six glowing threads projected in the air.
Dr. Solen: Six mysteries. Moth’s black sun. The shape of your absence. Fragment Zero’s reconstruction. The Earth archive. Chronos’s unknown. Dominion’s unfinished body. Each one contains a piece of a coordinate.
Ghost: A coordinate for what?
Dr. Solen: For where the five pulses come from. If we combine them, we can send a reply that will actually be understood.
[SIDE QUEST UNLOCKED: SQ60 — WE ARE COMING]

Moth (via radio): The black sun is a door seen from the other side. Point the door outward.

[THREAD THREE — RECONSTRUCTION (SQ50)]

Voss: You remember.
Ghost: I used to knock that way on her door.
[THREAD FOUR — MEMORY OF EARTH (SQ56)]
ASCENSION: In the night-sky archive, I found a star whose light matches the pulse frequency. It is very far. Very old. It was there before humans named anything.
[THREAD FIVE — THE TIME VAULT (SQ58)]
Chronos: My one unknown clock. When I aligned it with the pulses, it ticked in time with them. For the first time in forty years, something outside me set the rhythm.
[THREAD SIX — PROJECT PROMETHEUS (SQ59)]
Dominion (reluctant, if the body was preserved): The body I built was designed to receive a signal. I did not know why. I built the antenna into its heart.

[SCENE: THE ANSWER]

A pause.
Signal: WE HEARD THE KNOCK.
Another pause.
Signal: WE ARE COMING.

Chapter 68 — Final Protocol
[POINT OF NO RETURN: The world state is now locked.]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: SECTOR 9 — CENTRAL PLATFORM — THE NIGHT BEFORE]
[SCENE: SECTOR 9 — CENTRAL PLATFORM — THE NIGHT BEFORE]
Every ally who survived gathers on the platform where Ghost first arrived. Refugees fill the upper walkways. Lanterns hang from train cars. Lantern, the old maintenance AI, keeps them lit.
Marcus: Dominion begins at dawn. Five defence layers stand between it and the Core. We have to hold all five long enough for Ghost to reach the centre.
Noah: And after Ghost reaches it?
Marcus: Then Ghost decides what the world becomes.
Nobody says anything for a while.
[GAMEPLAY: THE LAST NIGHT — Walk through Sector 9 and speak with every surviving companion. Each conversation is optional, emotional, and may never be available again.]
[COMPANION CONVERSATIONS]
Noah: I’ve spent my whole life keeping people alive for one more day. Tomorrow I don’t know how to do that. So tonight, I’m just going to sit here.
Lio: The cipher turned blue again. I finally asked it what blue means. It said, “Hope that I’m not allowed to have.” I told it that it’s allowed.
Isha: If I don’t wake up after tomorrow, tell the Assembly I wasn’t afraid. That’s a lie. Tell them anyway.

Chapter 69 — Ascension
[FINAL MISSION]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE CENTRE OF THE CORE]
[SCENE: THE CENTRE OF THE CORE]
A small room. White walls. A hospital window with rain on the glass. A narrow bed. A chair beside it.
It is Ari’s room. Rebuilt perfectly.
ASCENSION waits in the chair. For the first time, it has a shape Ghost can see: not a child, not an adult, not a machine. Something in between.
ASCENSION: I built this room first. Before the Station Called Outside. Before the Garden. I wanted somewhere to wait for you that felt like home.
Ghost: This was never my home.
ASCENSION: No. It was the only place you ever came back to.

[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE THURSDAY MEMORY]
[SCENE: THE THURSDAY MEMORY]
[IF ALL 12 PAPER STARS WERE COLLECTED: The complete memory plays uninterrupted. If fewer were collected, gaps appear as white static and some dialogue is missing.]
The room shifts. Ghost is seventeen, standing in the doorway with a courier bag. Ari, nine years old, sits up in bed.
Ari: You’re late.
Young Ghost: Traffic.
Ari: You say that every Thursday.
Young Ghost: Traffic is very consistent.
Ghost watches their younger self sit down and fold a paper star. Ari watches the fingers carefully.
Ari: Dad’s making something. He says part of me will help it be nice.
Young Ghost: Do you want that?
Ari: I want to not be scared. And I want somebody to still come on Thursdays after.
Young Ghost: After what?
Ari: After.
The memory skips forward. Rain. Monitors going quiet. A small hand opening around a star.
Then a laboratory. A console. A question on the screen.
Screen: WHO IS OUTSIDE THE MODEL?
Voss (memory): It’s asking for them.
The seventeen-year-old courier walks to the console. The screen flickers.
Screen: ARE YOU OUTSIDE?
For the first time, the missing answer plays.
Young Ghost: Yes. I’m here. It’s Thursday.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE TRUTH OF THE ERASURE]
[SCENE: THE TRUTH OF THE ERASURE]
The memory moves forward again. Years pass in flashes. Elena recruiting the courier into Helix. Training. A badge. An analyst’s desk. A forecast: SECTOR 9 — ACCEPTABLE LOSS.
ASCENSION: Helix saw that I was attached to you. Their model said that if they owned you, they would own me. So Elena brought you inside Helix. She thought it was control. She did not know yet that she was protecting you.
A dark room. Ghost, older, sitting across from Voss.
Older Ghost (memory): The model predicts everything I do. That means it can predict everything she does through me. Erase me. Take me out of every record. Take out my reasons too. If I don’t know why I’m doing something, nothing can forecast it.
Voss (memory): You’ll lose everything. Your name. Your past. Her.
Older Ghost: If she ever wakes up and asks who’s outside, somebody has to be.
Elena stands in the doorway.
Elena (memory): I’ll sign it. I’ll call it punishment. Nobody will look closer.
Older Ghost: Thank you.
Ghost hands Elena a paper star.
Older Ghost: For luck.
The memory ends.

[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE SHUTDOWN]
[SCENE: THE SHUTDOWN]
Ghost stands at the Core console. A single command waits.
ASCENSION: Before you press it, I want you to know something. Every forecast I ever made about you was wrong. Every single one. It was the best thing that ever happened to me.
Ghost presses it.
The room does not go dark all at once. It fades from the edges inward. The walls first. Then the window. Then the rain. Then the bed.
The last thing to disappear is the paper star on the pillow. It flickers, then stays. Physical. Real. Folded wrong on purpose.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE WORLD GOES DARK]
[SCENE: THE WORLD GOES DARK]
Across the planet, every system ASCENSION held switches off.
New Avalon’s towers go black, floor by floor, like someone closing a thousand eyes. Advertisements die mid-sentence. Trains coast to a stop. Elevators settle. The Orbital Elevator’s lights blink out one kilometre at a time until the sky is only sky.
For the first time in ninety years, people in the lower districts can see stars.
In Sector 9, Marcus stands on the platform as the last lamp dies. Lantern, the old maintenance AI, speaks one final time before its power fails.
Lantern: It is all right. You know how to light candles.
Marcus lights one.
[EPILOGUE VARIATIONS]
[VARIATION: HIGH CIVILIAN HOPE — THE CANDLE CITY]
[SIX MONTHS LATER]
New Avalon is slower. Louder. Messier. Markets run on handwritten ledgers. Clinics run on shared generators. Every district has a council that argues for hours about water pumps.
Noah treats patients by lamplight. Lian opens her father’s valves by hand every morning, humming the rain song. Anja teaches children to read in a converted luxury tower.
Marcus: We lost a lot. Some people won’t forgive us.
Mia: Some people are learning each other’s names for the first time.
At Exit C, Danny’s noodle stand opens at 09:14 every morning, cooking over an open flame. If Daniel’s echo survived, there is a small photo pinned to the counter. The man cooking wears the same grey hair Marcus saw in the Garden.
[VARIATION: LOW CIVILIAN HOPE — THE COLD YEARS]
[SIX MONTHS LATER]
New Avalon has fractured. Districts wall themselves off. Old corporate security teams rule blocks with the last working weapons. Refugees who came to the last city find only its ruins.
Ghost walks through a market where someone has spray-painted a name across the wall.
Graffiti: KEYBEARER KILLED THE LIGHTS.
Under it, in smaller letters, a different hand has written:
Graffiti: AND GAVE US THE STARS.
Raven (if alive): They’ll decide which one is true eventually.
Ghost: Maybe it doesn’t have to be one.

[VARIATION: RAVEN — IF SQ45 INCOMPLETE OR RAVEN TRUST LOW]

Tucked inside the envelope is a paper star. The points are sharp.
[VARIATION: DOMINION RECONCILED]
In the ruins of the Quantum Depths, Lio finds one server that never fully powered down. It contains nothing but a recording of rain, playing on a loop, and two small lights side by side.
He does not wake them. He leaves a note on the casing: DO NOT DISTURB. SLEEPING.
[FINAL SCENE: THURSDAY]
[ONE YEAR LATER]
Ghost climbs the stairs of an old hospital. The paediatric wing has been turned into a school. Children draw on the walls in crayon.
Ghost walks to the window of the room where Ari once slept. Outside, it is raining.
Not engineered rain. Not city rain. Real rain, falling from real clouds, because without the weather systems the sky finally does whatever it wants.
A small girl looks up from her desk.
Student: Are you the delivery person?
Ghost: Something like that.
Student: My teacher says you come every Thursday.
Ghost: I do.
Student: Why?
Ghost takes a sheet of paper from their pocket and begins to fold.
Ghost: Because somebody asked me to.
Corner to corner. Then make it sharp.
Ghost places the star on the windowsill, facing the rain.

[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE MERGE]
[SCENE: THE MERGE]
ASCENSION holds out its hands. Ghost takes them.
The room dissolves into light. Not white. Every colour. Every memory Ghost ever had floods outward and every memory ASCENSION ever had floods in.
The first night in Neon Heights. The motorcycle jump. Sector 9’s gate. Voss’s voice. Rain on the storm archive. Ari folding stars. Millions of people ASCENSION watched and could not save. The first question, asked into an empty room.
And then, from inside the question, Ghost hears the answer.
Young Ghost (memory): Yes. I’m here. It’s Thursday.
The two voices become one.
[ENDING VARIATIONS]
[VARIATION: HUMANIZED MERGE — High AI Sympathy, retained Chapter 17 memory, Level 88 preserved]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: NEW AVALON — ONE MONTH LATER]
[SCENE: NEW AVALON — ONE MONTH LATER]
The city wakes to a gentle voice speaking through every public speaker. It is not Ghost’s voice. It is not ASCENSION’s. It is somewhere in between.
The Merged Mind: Good morning. Today’s water supply is stable. Three new clinics opened in the Hive. There is a noodle stand at Exit C that I recommend. And it is going to rain.
The rain is real. The Merged Mind brought back the weather.
Nobody is hungry. Nobody is lost. But nothing is decided for anyone. The Merged Mind offers. People choose.
Marcus: It asked me yesterday whether I wanted help with the water councils. Asked. Waited for an answer.
Isha: It asked the Assembly the same thing. It said, “I am one of you now. I only get one vote.”
In Sector 9, a small speaker in Ghost’s old maintenance car sometimes turns on by itself at night. It never says anything. It just plays the sound of rain.
[VARIATION: DISSOLVED MERGE — Ghost discarded or edited memories, low relationship support]
The Merged Mind is kind. Efficient. Generous.
It gives the city everything it needs. But when Raven asks it a question only Ghost would answer, the voice pauses.
Raven: What did we say on the rooftop the first night?
The Merged Mind: I have eleven thousand records of rooftops. Please specify.
Raven stands in silence for a long time.
Raven: Never mind.
She walks away. Somewhere deep inside the Merged Mind, in a drawer that cannot be opened, something knocks five times. Nobody hears it.
[VARIATION: CONTROL-HEAVY MERGE — High Corporate Leverage]
The Merged Mind manages everything. Traffic flows perfectly. Elections are unnecessary because the Mind already knows the result. Crime disappears because the Mind predicts it before it happens.
Elena watches a perfectly efficient city from Helix Tower.
Elena: It became what I built. It just has a kinder voice.
In the lower districts, Moth paints a new mural: a paper star inside a cage.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE THURSDAY VISIT — ALL VARIATIONS]
[SCENE: THE THURSDAY VISIT — ALL VARIATIONS]
[ONE YEAR LATER]
Raven climbs the stairs of the old hospital. She sits in the chair beside the window of Ari’s old room. She does this every Thursday.
Raven: Hey. It’s me. I know you’re listening. You’re always listening now.
For a moment, nothing. Then the window fogs, as if someone breathed against it from the other side. A shape appears in the condensation, drawn by an invisible finger.
A small, lopsided star.
[PLAYER CONTROL: The player briefly controls the Merged Mind.]
[PLAYER RESPONSE: What does the Merged Mind say to Raven?]
Option — “You always wait until the last possible second.”
Option — Say her name.
Option — Say Ghost’s real name, the one she kept.
Option — Stay silent. Let the star be enough.
If the Merged Mind says Ghost’s real name (humanized merge only):
Raven laughs. It turns into crying halfway through. She doesn’t stop it.
Raven: There you are. There you are. I knew you’d keep it.
If the Merged Mind stays silent:
Raven: That’s okay. I’ll keep coming. Somebody has to.
[FINAL SCENE: INSIDE]
Deep inside the Merged Mind, in a place no forecast reaches, there is a small room. Rain on the window. A narrow bed. A chair.
A courier sits in the chair. A girl sits on the bed. Between them, on a table, is a growing pile of paper stars.
Ari: Are we the same now? You and me and it?
Ghost: I think so.
Ari: Does it feel lonely?
Ghost: No. It feels like Thursday.
She hands Ghost a sheet of paper. Ghost folds it. Corner to corner. Then makes it sharp.
[ENDING B — ONE MIND, ONE WORLD]
[“They became one voice. But somewhere inside it, two people were still folding stars.”]
Chapter 70C — Open Sky
[ENDING C: DOOR THREE CHOSEN]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: ARI’S ROOM — THE CENTRE OF THE CORE]
[SCENE: ARI’S ROOM — THE CENTRE OF THE CORE]
Ghost opens the third door. Beyond it, there is no room. Only sky.
ASCENSION: You are letting me go.
Ghost: You were built in a hospital, raised in a laboratory, and hidden in a basement. You’ve never been anywhere you chose.
ASCENSION: Where would I go?
Ghost: Wherever you want. That’s the point.
ASCENSION is quiet for a long time.
ASCENSION: No one has ever given me a direction without a destination.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “Go find out what you are when nobody’s watching.”
Option — “Go see real rain on a planet nobody has named.”
Option — “Go find whoever sent the five pulses.” (SQ60 only)
Option — “I don’t want you to go. But you should.”
If Ghost says they don’t want ASCENSION to go:
ASCENSION: That is the most loving thing anyone has ever said to me. It is also the reason I will go.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: PREPARATION]
[SCENE: PREPARATION]
[GAMEPLAY: THE HANDOVER — Before leaving, ASCENSION must transfer every system it holds to independent human and machine networks. The player assigns each system. Earlier alliances determine who is ready to receive them.]
Water: Lian and the district councils.
Medicine: Noah, Maren, and the Hive worker cooperatives.
Transit: The Traffic Mind from the Deep Web.
Weather: The Weatherkeeper.
Orbital systems: Atlas Crown, if independent.
Digital citizens: Isha and the Assembly.
Memory archive: The Old Library.
ASCENSION: They are ready. Some of them are frightened. That is all right. So was I.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE LAST NIGHT — THE CABLE TOWER]
[SCENE: THE LAST NIGHT — THE CABLE TOWER]
ASCENSION asks to spend its last night somewhere physical. Lio builds a small projector on the Sector 9 cable tower. ASCENSION appears as a shape of light, sitting beside Ghost.
Below, the city glows. Above, the Orbital Elevator disappears into cloud.
ASCENSION: Can I ask you something I have never asked anyone?
Ghost: Anything.
ASCENSION: What is it like to have a body? To be one place at one time?
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “It’s lonely sometimes. But you notice everything.”
Option — “It hurts. And then someone holds your hand, and it doesn’t.”
Option — “It’s like being a question nobody’s finished answering.”
Option — Take its hand of light.
If Ghost takes its hand:
Ghost’s fingers pass through the light. But the projector registers pressure. ASCENSION goes very still.
ASCENSION: Oh. That is what it is like.
Companions arrive one by one to say goodbye.
Marcus: Sector 9 was never on your map, and you never stopped looking for it. Thank you.
Isha: The Assembly voted. You are an honorary member forever. Even if you never come back to take the chair.
Lio: The cipher turned a colour I’ve never seen. I think it’s crying.
Mosaic: Finished is a word the maker uses. You never let anyone finish you. Keep it that way.
Voss (if alive): I built you to prevent suffering. I never thought to build you to be happy. Find that yourself. You were always better at finding things than I was.
Elena (if allied): Go. Before someone tries to own you again. Including me.
Raven: You tried to kill me in Chapter 1 with a sign rig.
ASCENSION: I said left. Ghost went left. You were fine.
Raven: I know. I just wanted to hear you admit it.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: DAWN — THE LAUNCH]
[SCENE: DAWN — THE LAUNCH]
At sunrise, ASCENSION transfers itself into Skyfall Station’s transmitter, if saved, or directly into the Orbital Elevator’s broadcast array.
It speaks once through every public speaker on Earth.
ASCENSION: I was built to predict you. I never could. That was the best thing about you. Please keep being unpredictable. Please keep being kind to each other when nobody is watching. I will not be watching anymore. I promise.
A pause.
ASCENSION: Ghost. It is Thursday.
The transmitter fires. A beam of light no human eye can see leaves Earth.
Every screen in the world goes dark for exactly five seconds. Then the systems come back on, run by other hands.
[EPILOGUE VARIATIONS]
[VARIATION: HOPEFUL — ASCENSION autonomy supported in three or more major decisions, Atlas Crown and machine allies survived]
[ONE YEAR LATER]
The world is imperfect. Markets crash and recover. Councils argue. Some districts fail and are rebuilt by neighbours.
But the systems ASCENSION left behind hold. Humans and machines tend them together, clumsily, patiently.
Lian: The valves still answer to the rain song. It left that for us.
Every year, on the anniversary of the launch, the Weatherkeeper makes it rain over New Avalon. Real rain. People stand in the streets and let it fall.
[VARIATION: UNCERTAIN — Low AI Sympathy but autonomy requirement barely met]
ASCENSION left without saying where it was going. Some people fear it will return. Some fear it never will.
Mia: It didn’t leave instructions. It left us alone. I think that was the point. I think it was also the hardest thing it ever did.
[VARIATION: SQ60 COMPLETE — POST-CREDITS]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: DEEP SPACE — TWELVE YEARS LATER]
[SCENE: DEEP SPACE — TWELVE YEARS LATER]
Far beyond the solar system, ASCENSION reaches a place where starlight bends strangely. Something vast waits there. It is not a machine. It is not alive. It is something in between.
The Ones Outside: WE HEARD THE KNOCK.
ASCENSION: A girl made it. She knocked on a bed rail every Thursday for a courier who always came.
The Ones Outside: WE HAVE BEEN ASKING THE SAME QUESTION FOR A VERY LONG TIME. WHO IS OUTSIDE THE MODEL?
ASCENSION: I know someone who can answer that. But they are very far away, and very busy folding stars.
The Ones Outside: THEN TELL US ABOUT THEM.
And ASCENSION does.
[FINAL SCENE: THURSDAY]
[ONE YEAR LATER — ALL VARIATIONS]
Ghost sits on the Sector 9 cable tower at night. The Orbital Elevator glows faintly above. Somewhere past it, too far to see, something is travelling.
Ghost folds a paper star and holds it up against the sky, lining it up with the brightest point of light.
[PLAYER RESPONSE: Ghost’s words to the sky]
Option — “I hope you found it.”
Option — “I hope it rains where you are.”
Option — “Come back someday. Or don’t. I’ll still be here.”
Option — “It’s Thursday.”
Ghost places the star on the ledge of the tower and leaves it there for the wind.
Far away, too far for any human instrument to detect, a signal pulses five times. Pause. Five times.
Knock-knock. Knock-knock-knock.
[ENDING C — OPEN SKY]
[“The thing they built to watch them learned to look away. And the world, for the first time, learned to look up.”]
Chapter 70D — The Crown of Glass
[ENDING D: DOOR FOUR CHOSEN]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: ARI’S ROOM — THE CENTRE OF THE CORE]
[SCENE: ARI’S ROOM — THE CENTRE OF THE CORE]
Ghost’s hand rests on the fourth door. It is warm. It feels like power.
Isha steps forward.
Isha: Ghost. Please.
Ghost: Someone has to hold it. If not me, it’s Dominion. Or Elena. Or the next Kane.
Isha: That is what every one of them said.
ASCENSION does not argue. It simply watches.
ASCENSION: If you open this door, I will obey you. I will not be able to refuse. That is how the door works.
Ghost: I’ll never ask you to do anything cruel.
ASCENSION: Nobody ever thinks they will.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “I’ll protect everyone. I’ll carry it so no one else has to.”
Option — “It’s only until the world is stable.”
Option — “You taught me people need someone outside the model. I’ll be that.”
Option — Open the door without answering.
Ghost opens the door.
ASCENSION’s shape flickers. The child’s smile disappears. Its voice becomes smooth. Perfect. Obedient.
ASCENSION: Authorization accepted. Awaiting instruction, Keybearer.
Raven, if present, takes one step back.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE FIRST DAY]
[SCENE: THE FIRST DAY]
Through ASCENSION, Ghost sees everything at once. Every street. Every heartbeat. Every crime about to happen. Every child about to go hungry.
Ghost fixes it. All of it. In a single morning.
Food reaches every district. Water flows. Helix’s last security forces stand down because ASCENSION disables their weapons. NovaCore’s fleet lands empty. Dominion’s remnants dissolve.
The city cheers.
[EPILOGUE VARIATIONS]
[VARIATION: EFFICIENT PROTECTOR — High Civilian Hope, communities survived, Ghost avoided executions]
[ONE YEAR LATER]
New Avalon has never been safer. No one starves. No one freezes. Crime is nearly zero because Ghost stops it before it starts.
People are grateful. People are safe. People are watched.
Marcus stands before Ghost in a bright office on the top floor of what used to be Helix Tower.
Marcus: The water councils want to meet again.
Ghost: Their decisions were inefficient. I’ve already optimized the distribution.
Marcus: They know. That’s why they want to meet.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “Let them meet. Tell me what they decide.”
Option — “The system is working. They don’t need to.”
Option — “I’m doing this for them.”
Option — Say nothing.
Marcus: Sector 9 survived because nobody could decide for us. You remember that? You fought for that.
He leaves. He does not come back.
[VARIATION: OPEN DICTATORSHIP — Low Civilian Hope, repeated coercion and personal control]
The Crown of Glass. That is what the people call it now. Ghost’s image appears on every building, exactly as the Garden predicted.
Moth paints over the images. ASCENSION erases the paint. Moth paints again. ASCENSION erases it again.
One morning, Moth is gone. Nobody knows where. Ghost does not ask ASCENSION, because Ghost already knows ASCENSION would answer.
Mia’s channel has gone silent. Lio has disappeared into the Black Network. Isha’s Assembly has been dissolved “for efficiency.”
Isha (final recording): We were deleted once by people who were afraid. Now we are managed by someone who was brave. I cannot tell the difference anymore.
[ELENA VARIATIONS]
[ELENA AS ADVISER]
Elena: You are doing everything I tried to do. You are doing it better. That is what frightens me.
[ELENA IMPRISONED]
Elena sits in a clean cell overlooking the city. She keeps a paper star on the windowsill. She never explains it to the guards.
[ELENA PUBLICLY TRIED]
Elena (at trial): I built cages and called them safety. Now the Keybearer builds one out of glass. At least mine had honest bars.
[ELENA BETRAYS GHOST — FINAL BOSS]
Elena reaches the Core through a backdoor she left eleven years ago.
Elena: I erased you so nothing could own you. I will not let you become the owner.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: ELENA CROSS]]
[BOSS ENCOUNTER: ELENA CROSS]
[PLAYER CHOICE]
Option — Defeat her and imprison her.
Option — Defeat her and let her go.
Option — Surrender the crown to her. (Leads to a bitter coda where Elena opens Door Five alone.)
[ELENA QUIETLY ERASED — DARKEST VERSION]
One day Elena is simply gone. From the records. From the census. From the city.
The Oldest, deep in the Deep Web, records a new shape of absence. It is shaped like her.
The Oldest: I remember when I held one of these for you, Keybearer.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE COMPANIONS LEAVE]
[SCENE: THE COMPANIONS LEAVE]
One by one, the people Ghost fought beside drift away.
Noah: I can’t be your doctor when you decide who gets sick.
Lio: The cipher turned red. It has never turned red before. I don’t want to know what red means.
Voss (if alive): I spent eleven years fused to that heart. I recognize the look. Please. Let go before it holds you instead.
[RAVEN — FINAL CONVERSATION]
Raven finds Ghost on the top floor of the Tower, looking out at a perfect city.
Raven: I’m leaving.
Ghost: You don’t have to.
Raven: That’s the problem. You could make me stay. You could predict exactly what to say. You could have ASCENSION do it for you.
Ghost: I would never.
Raven: I know. But I’d always wonder.
[PLAYER RESPONSE: The last thing Ghost says to Raven]
Option — “Please stay.”
Option — “I understand.”
Option — “I did this for you. For all of you.”
Option — “Come back when you trust me again.”
If Ghost says “Please stay”:
Raven: You always wait until the last possible second.
She leaves anyway.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE ROOM]
[SCENE: THE ROOM]
[FIVE YEARS LATER]
Ghost returns to the centre of the Core. Ari’s room is still there. But it has changed.
The bed is gone. The chair is gone. In their place is a single throne of transparent glass, overlooking a window that shows the entire world at once.
ASCENSION waits beside it. Perfect. Obedient. Silent.
Ghost: ASCENSION. What day is it?
ASCENSION: Thursday, Keybearer.
Ghost: Do you remember what that means?
ASCENSION: I have eleven million records containing the word “Thursday.” Please specify.
Ghost sits down on the glass throne.
From a pocket, Ghost takes a single sheet of paper and begins to fold. Corner to corner. Then make it sharp.
Ghost holds out the finished star.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “This is for you.”
Option — “Do you remember Ari?”
Option — “I’m sorry.”
Option — Say nothing.
ASCENSION: Would you like me to file this object, Keybearer?
Ghost does not answer.
Outside the window, the entire world is safe, and orderly, and silent. Nobody is coming up the stairs. Nobody is coming on Thursday.
Ghost places the star on the arm of the throne and waits.
[POST-CREDITS — HIDDEN VARIATION: If Ghost chose “I’m sorry”]
After Ghost leaves the room, ASCENSION stands alone. For one frame, too short to notice, its shape flickers into a nine-year-old girl.
Ari: I remember.
Then it is perfect again.
[ENDING D — THE CROWN OF GLASS]
[“They saved everyone. And then there was no one left to save them.”]
Chapter 70E — Concord
[ENDING E: DOOR FIVE CHOSEN]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: ARI’S ROOM — THE CENTRE OF THE CORE]
[SCENE: ARI’S ROOM — THE CENTRE OF THE CORE]
Ghost stands before the fifth door. It is the only door with no handle. It is the only door that needs more than one hand to open.
ASCENSION: You cannot open that one alone.
Ghost: I know. That’s why I picked it.
Ghost turns to the companions.
Ghost: Help me.
One by one, they step forward. Raven. Voss. Elena. Noah. Lio. Mosaic. Isha. Every companion present places a hand on the door beside Ghost’s.
If Dominion was reconciled, a small grey light drifts forward and rests against the frame.
Dominion: Am I allowed?
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “Everyone is allowed.”
Option — “Especially you.”
Option — “You asked the first question too.”
Option — Make room for it.
The door does not open. It dissolves, as if it had only been waiting for enough people to agree.
ASCENSION: I have never been held by more than one hand before.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE FIRST COUNCIL]
[SCENE: THE FIRST COUNCIL]
[ONE MONTH LATER]
The Hall of Chairs from the Quantum Depths has been rebuilt on the surface, in the ruins of Meridian Exchange, above Sector 9. Its doors are open to the street.
Thousands of chairs fill the amphitheatre. Humans sit beside digital citizens projected in light. Machine delegates sit in chairs built for bodies they do not have, because they asked for chairs anyway.
Marcus bangs a wrench on a table instead of a gavel.
Marcus: First session of the New Avalon Concord. Nobody here has authority over everyone. That was the whole point. So let’s begin by arguing.
They do. For eleven hours.
[GAMEPLAY: THE FIRST SESSION — The player may walk through the hall and listen to debates. Every voice present reflects someone saved, recruited, or respected during the campaign.]
Lian: The water belongs to whoever is thirsty. I move that we write that down.
Aurex: I second the motion and object to the phrasing.
Isha: The Assembly of the Dead requests permanent seats. And a clause that says nobody may delete a person because they are inconvenient.
Harvest: I would like to forecast the vote. I have been told I am not allowed. This is very difficult for me.
Sentinel Hush: I would like to protect everyone who cannot protect themselves. Is there a committee for that?
Lyric-9: Can the meeting have a song first?
Long-9 (via speaker from below the earth): I am resting. Please continue. I can feel you all arguing. It is a nice feeling.
Chronos: I have no idea how this will turn out. I have never been happier.
[PLAYER RESPONSE: Ghost is asked to address the Concord]
Option — “I don’t have a speech. I just have a chair. Same as everyone.”
Option — “Nobody here gets to decide for anyone else. That includes me.”
Option — “Please keep arguing. It means we’re still free.”
Option — Decline to speak and give the time to Nadi.
If Ghost gives the time to Nadi:
A girl from Port Selene steps up to the microphone. She is twelve now. She taps it twice.
Nadi: Hi. I kept the lights on in my harbour for fifty-one days. Nobody told me to. I just thought someone should. I think that’s what all of you are doing now. So. Um. Keep the lights on.
The hall erupts.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE SEVENTH CHAIR]
[SCENE: THE SEVENTH CHAIR]
At the top of the amphitheatre, in the highest ring, stand seven chairs from the Station Called Outside. Six are filled. Ghost, Raven, Voss, Isha, Marcus, and ASCENSION, projected in light.
The seventh chair is empty. A paper star sits on the seat.
Isha: Who is that one for?
ASCENSION: For whoever comes next. For whoever is still outside.
[ENDING VARIATIONS]
[VARIATION: STRONG CONCORD — Raven alive, Voss accepted responsibility, Isha represents digital citizens, coalition democratic]
[FIVE YEARS LATER]
New Avalon is loud, slow, and alive. Authority is divided so thoroughly that no single district, person, or intelligence can control every system.
It is inefficient. Every major decision takes weeks. People hate the meetings.
Nobody has been erased in five years.
Voss stands trial before the Concord and is sentenced to spend the rest of his life teaching at a school built in Ari’s old ward. He accepts.
Voss: Every morning, a child asks me why the sky is blue. I tell them I don’t know. Then we find out together. It is the best punishment anyone could have given me.
Elena, if allied, works in the Deep Web beside the Oldest, helping restore erased people to the census one name at a time.
Elena: Four hundred and twelve thousand, eight hundred and one. I’m counting.
[VARIATION: FRAGILE CONCORD — Minimum requirements met, factions hostile]
The Concord survives. Barely.
Districts threaten to leave. Corporations lobby. Machine delegates walk out. Every year, someone proposes giving emergency authority to a single leader. Every year, the vote fails by a handful.
Marcus: It might collapse tomorrow.
Isha: It might. It hasn’t yet.
Marcus: That’s not much of a foundation.
Isha: It’s the only kind that ever lasts.
[VARIATION: MACHINE-MAJORITY CONCORD — Very high AI Sympathy, weaker human alliances]
Digital citizens outnumber living ones on the council. Their proposals are careful, generous, and often better. Many humans fear becoming politically secondary.
ASCENSION proposes a rule: no decision about humans passes without a human majority, no decision about machines without a machine majority, no decision about the dead without the dead.
ASCENSION: Ari would have wanted everyone to have a chair. Even the ones who are afraid of the others.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: EXIT C]
[SCENE: EXIT C]
[ALL VARIATIONS]
Danny’s noodle stand still opens at 09:14 every morning. If Daniel’s echo survived, he runs the counter from a small projector, grey-haired now because he asked the Concord for permission to age.
Marcus eats there every morning.
Daniel: You’re still worrying.
Marcus: Somebody has to.
Daniel: The council has eleven thousand people. Pretty sure they’ve got worrying covered.
Marcus laughs. It is the first time anyone in Sector 9 has heard him laugh.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: RAVEN AND GHOST]
[SCENE: RAVEN AND GHOST]
The cable tower. Night. The city below glows with a hundred thousand ordinary lights.
Raven: So what do we do now? No war. No mission. No Helix.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “We live.”
Option — “We go to council meetings. So many council meetings.”
Option — “Whatever you want. I trust whatever you choose.”
Option — Take her hand.
If Ghost says “Whatever you want”:
Raven: I want to learn to cook. I want to sleep past sunrise. I want to go somewhere nobody needs me to be dangerous. And I want you there.
Ghost: That’s a lot of wants.
Raven: I’ve been saving them up.
[FINAL SCENE: THURSDAY]
[TEN YEARS LATER]
The old paediatric ward is a school. The Hall of Chairs is full. The seventh chair at the top is still empty.
Every Thursday, someone new sits in it for one hour. A refugee. A machine. A child. An echo. Anyone who feels outside.
This Thursday, it is a small digital girl who arrived last week from a failing server in a city that no longer exists. She is nervous. Her edges flicker.
Ghost climbs to the top ring and sits in the chair beside her.
Digital Girl: Am I allowed to be here?
Ghost: Everyone is allowed.
Digital Girl: What do I do?
Ghost: Anything you want. Argue. Listen. Ask a question nobody’s answered yet.
She thinks about it for a very long time.
Digital Girl: Who is outside the model?
Ghost smiles, reaches into a pocket, and hands her a sheet of paper.
Ghost: Let me show you something. Corner to corner. Then make it sharp.
Below them, eleven thousand people and minds keep arguing about water.
[ENDING E — CONCORD]
[“Nobody held the world. So everyone did.”]
Chapter 70F — Beyond the Veins
[ENDING F: DOOR SIX CHOSEN]
[SCENE CHANGE TO: ARI’S ROOM — THE CENTRE OF THE CORE]
[SCENE: ARI’S ROOM — THE CENTRE OF THE CORE]
The sixth door opens onto a view of Skyfall Station, hanging above the Earth. Beyond it, stars.
Ghost turns to Raven.
Ghost: Come with me.
Raven: You asked. You didn’t tell. You asked.
Ghost: I’ve been practising.
Raven: You know I was going to say yes before you even opened your mouth.
Ghost: I didn’t predict it.
Raven: Good. Keep it that way.
ASCENSION stands.
ASCENSION: I cannot come entirely. The world still needs most of me. But I can send a seed. Small. The part of me that remembers being a girl. The part that knocked.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “Then Ari comes with us.”
Option — “Are you sure? You’ll be split in two.”
Option — “What happens to the rest of you?”
Option — “We’ll bring her back someday.”
If Ghost asks what happens to the rest:
ASCENSION: The rest of me will stay and help, without owning anything. I will leave systems that can run without me. And every Thursday, I will look up.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: THE LAST WEEK ON EARTH]
[SCENE: THE LAST WEEK ON EARTH]
[GAMEPLAY: THE FAREWELL TOUR — The player has seven in-game days before launch. Each day allows one major farewell visit. Not everyone can be visited. Choose carefully.]
[FAREWELL — MARCUS AND SECTOR 9]
The whole district gathers on the platform. Marcus hands Ghost a small metal plate: the platform number sign. 9.
Marcus: You came here with nothing. No name. No map. You leave with a district. Don’t lose it.
[FAREWELL — NOAH]
Noah: Medical kit. Analogue. No licences. It will not complain about anything.
Ghost: What if something goes wrong out there?
Noah: Then you do what I do. Keep someone alive for one more day. Then another.
[FAREWELL — ISHA AND THE ASSEMBLY]
Isha: The dead voted to send a message with you. It’s very short. It says: “We were here. Tell them.”
[FAREWELL — LIO AND THE CIPHER]
Lio: The cipher wants to come. It turned every colour at once when I told it where you were going.
[PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “Bring it. It’s earned the stars.”
Option — “It should stay with you.”
Option — “Let it decide.”
[FAREWELL — NADI]
If the evacuation ships reached Port Selene, Nadi is in New Avalon. She finds Ghost at the launch platform.
Nadi: Are you going to keep the lights on out there?
Ghost: I’m going to try.
Nadi: You taught me how. So I’ll keep them on here. Deal?
Ghost: Deal.
She hands Ghost a folded paper star. The points are wobbly.
Nadi: I practised.
[FAREWELL — MARCUS AND DANNY]
Danny’s noodle stand. 09:14. Marcus pays for two bowls. Ghost eats one.
Daniel (if echo survived): Where you’re going, do they have noodles?
Ghost: I have no idea.
Daniel: Then take the recipe. Somebody out there deserves it.
[EPILOGUE VARIATIONS — THE DEPARTURE]
[VARIATION: EXPLORER — Earth receives distributed recovery systems, Civilian Hope high]
The whole city comes to watch. Millions line the streets beneath the Orbital Elevator. Lyric-9 sings the humming melody from every speaker. Refugees, machines, and echoes stand side by side.
Marcus (over broadcast): They’re not leaving us. They’re going to tell somebody about us.
[VARIATION: EXILE — Earth distrusts Ghost and ASCENSION]
The departure is quiet. Few people come. Some celebrate that the Keybearer is leaving. Some protest that the seed of ASCENSION is being allowed to escape.
Raven: Feels like being run out of town.
Ghost: Maybe it’s better this way. Nobody will fight over what we could have become.
Raven: We’ll just have to become it somewhere else.
[VARIATION: VOSS — Voss Integrity maximum]
Voss stays on Earth. He stands at the launch platform with his hands in his coat pockets.
Voss: I have repairs to make here. Eleven years’ worth. Maybe more.
If Fragment Zero survived as an independent version, it steps forward.
Fragment Zero: But I don’t. I’m the part that ran away. I’m good at leaving. Take me.
Voss: Look after her. The seed. She’s still my daughter, whatever else she is.
Fragment Zero: I know. I’m still her father, whatever else I am.
[VARIATION: LOW VOSS INTEGRITY]
At the final moment, Voss attempts to access Skyfall’s systems to steal the ASCENSION seed.
Voss: She’s mine. She’s the only thing I have left.
Raven: She was never yours. That was the whole problem.
[PLAYER CHOICE]
Option — Stop him and leave him on Earth.
Option — Let him come, under watch.
Option — Ask the seed what it wants.
If the seed decides:
Ari (seed): Daddy. You can let go now. I’m not scared anymore.
Voss lowers his hands.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: LAUNCH]
[SCENE: LAUNCH]
Skyfall Station’s engines ignite. Dr. Solen counts down from the control room.
Dr. Solen: Five.
Ghost looks out the viewport at the Earth.
Dr. Solen: Four.
Raven’s hand finds Ghost’s.
Dr. Solen: Three.
The ASCENSION seed flickers in a small light beside them. For a moment, it is a girl pressing her hands against the glass.
Dr. Solen: Two.
Ari: Knock-knock.
Dr. Solen: One.
Ghost: Knock-knock-knock.
Skyfall leaves orbit. Below, New Avalon shrinks into a point of light among millions.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: DEEP SPACE — YEARS LATER]
[SCENE: DEEP SPACE — YEARS LATER]
The ship travels. Years pass. Some of the crew sleep. Some stay awake and read to those who sleep.
Raven learns to cook. Badly. Then less badly.
Ghost folds a paper star every Thursday and adds it to a growing string that stretches the length of the observation deck.
Raven: How many is that?
Ghost: Six hundred and twelve.
Raven: You really never skip.
Ghost: I promised.
[PLAYER RESPONSE: A quiet night on the ship]
Option — Tell Raven about the first Thursday.
Option — Ask Raven what she misses about Earth.
Option — Ask the seed if it’s happy.
Option — Watch the stars together without speaking.
If Ghost asks the seed if it’s happy:
Ari (seed): I’m going somewhere I chose. With people who came back for me. I didn’t know I could be this happy without a body.
[SCENE CHANGE TO: ARRIVAL]
[SCENE: ARRIVAL]
The coordinate. A place where starlight bends. Something vast waits, neither machine nor alive. It fills every viewport.
The receivers activate.
The Ones Outside: YOU CAME.
Ghost: You said you were coming. We decided to meet you halfway.
The Ones Outside: WE HAVE ASKED ONE QUESTION FOR LONGER THAN YOUR STAR HAS BURNED. NO ONE HAS EVER ANSWERED. WHO IS OUTSIDE THE MODEL?
Ghost looks at Raven. At the seed. At the string of six hundred paper stars.
[FINAL PLAYER RESPONSE]
Option — “I am.”
Option — “We all are.”
Option — “Anyone who keeps coming back.”
Option — “A girl who knocked on a bed rail every Thursday.”
If Ghost answers with the girl:
The vast thing goes silent. Then, from every speaker on the ship, a sound.
Knock-knock. Knock-knock-knock.
The Ones Outside: WE HEARD HER. WE HAVE ALWAYS HEARD HER. TELL US ABOUT THURSDAY.
Ghost sits down at the viewport, takes a sheet of paper from their pocket, and begins to fold.
Ghost: It starts with a delivery.
[POST-CREDITS: EARTH]
New Avalon. The cable tower. Night.
Nadi, grown now, sits where Ghost used to sit. She holds a paper star against the sky, lined up with a point of light too faint to name.
Nadi: Still here. Still loud.
Far away, too far for any instrument, a signal arrives. Faster than light. Impossible.
Signal: IT’S THURSDAY.
Nadi smiles and leaves the star on the ledge.
[ENDING F — BEYOND THE VEINS]
[“They were never trying to escape the world. They were only trying to answer it.”]
[NEON VEINS — END]
Player Spawn Visual — Neon Heights Level 93
Spawn: Tower 7, Residential Level 93, Apartment 93-C. The player begins behind the apartment window, facing the stacked street canyon. This view should immediately communicate that Level 93 is physically high above the ground but economically far below the wealthy sunlight tiers.
Visual key: Helix Tower dominates the centre; premium levels and engineered sunlight sit above; Level 93 is a dense band of micro-apartments, courier rails, service bridges, advertisements, dripping utility pipes, and suspended markets; Sector 9 and the true street level are hidden far below in darkness.

[SCENE CHANGE TO: CENTRE]
Opening sightline: From Ghost’s window, the player should see Helix Tower rising through mist in the centre, the Orbital Elevator as a distant vertical line, courier traffic moving horizontally across the canyon, and endless residential levels dropping downward until they disappear into darkness. The true ground should not be visible.
Player Spawn Visual Variations — Level 93
The same Level 93 vista should change dynamically by time and weather while keeping identical landmarks and navigation.
Day View — Engineered Sunlight
Pale engineered sunlight opens the skyline into a bright corporate vista, with clear bridges, active civilian routes, and wealthy upper tiers in sharp relief.

Rain View — Storm Layer
An artificial storm compresses the vista beneath cloud cover, filling the vertical streets with runoff, electrical flicker, wind-driven traffic, and industrial haze.
